# Research Agent 4 — External Dependencies & Integration Seams (QDS-MEM-003 / Track A)

**MCP status:** Context7 was available and used for sqlite-vec and transformers.js. Hindsight, pi-mono, and swarmclaw facts come from real local source (not Context7). No fallbacks were forced — all sources resolved.

**Load-bearing context for the writers:** the operational-memory substrate is **vendored by copy-paste** from `ArtemisAI/pi-mem` (claude-mem v10.5.2 lineage, Bun/Node dual, `bun:sqlite`), capture layer stripped. The pi-mem source is **NOT present on this machine** (`find` for `pi-mem` returned nothing; it lives at the GitHub URL only). The closest *working reference implementation* of the sqlite + embeddings + FTS5 hybrid-search pattern that exists locally is **swarmclaw's `memory-db.ts` + `embeddings.ts`** — and it already uses `Xenova/all-MiniLM-L6-v2`. Stories should treat that pair as the canonical local template. Citations below.

---

## PART A — External Dependency Table

### A.1 sqlite-vec — P2.1 (`event_vec` virtual table, D3), SPIKE-11

| Field | Fact |
|---|---|
| Package / version | `sqlite-vec` (npm). Pure-C extension, loads into `better-sqlite3` / `bun:sqlite` via `sqliteVec.load(db)`. Pin an exact version in `package.json` at P2.0 (toolchain bootstrap, plan L132). Latest stable line is 0.1.x — confirm/pin during SPIKE-11; verify at runtime with `select vec_version()`. |
| API the story uses | `CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE … USING vec0(... float[384] ...)`; insert vectors as JSON string or compact `Float32` BLOB; KNN via `WHERE embedding MATCH :q AND k = N ORDER BY distance`. |
| Example | ```js\nimport * as sqliteVec from "sqlite-vec";\nimport Database from "better-sqlite3";\nconst db = new Database("events.db");\nsqliteVec.load(db);\ndb.exec(`CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE event_vec USING vec0(\n  event_id integer primary key,\n  embedding float[384]\n);`);\nconst f32 = new Float32Array(embedding); // 384 dims\ndb.prepare("INSERT INTO event_vec(event_id, embedding) VALUES (?, ?)")\n  .run(eventId, Buffer.from(f32.buffer));\nconst rows = db.prepare(`SELECT event_id, distance FROM event_vec\n  WHERE embedding MATCH ? AND k = ? ORDER BY distance`)\n  .all(Buffer.from(f32.buffer), 10);\n``` |
| Gotchas | (1) **KNN requires `k = ?`** (or `LIMIT`) in the WHERE clause — a vec0 MATCH query without `k`/`LIMIT` errors. (2) Dimension is fixed at table-create time as `float[384]`; mismatched insert length throws. (3) Default distance is **L2**; declare cosine explicitly if needed (`distance_metric=cosine`) — but since transformers.js output is already L2-normalized (`normalize: true`), L2 and cosine rank identically, so L2 on normalized vectors is fine. (4) **Partition key columns** (max 4, e.g. `variant text partition key`, `run_id`) shard the index and let A/B/run-scoped queries pre-filter — directly relevant to D2's `run_id` spine and P2.10 variant tagging. Aim for hundreds of vectors per partition value (over-sharding hurts). (5) Loading a native extension under Bun vs Node differs — this is precisely what SPIKE-11 must de-risk. |

Source: Context7 `/asg017/sqlite-vec` (`site/using/js.md`, `site/features/vec0.md`, `site/features/knn.md`).

### A.2 transformers.js / ONNX — `Xenova/all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, 384-dim — P2.1/P2.3, SPIKE-11, INV-OM4

| Field | Fact |
|---|---|
| Package / version | `@huggingface/transformers` (transformers.js v3 — the Xenova models are now under this scope). **swarmclaw pins `"@huggingface/transformers": "^3.8.1"`** (`/Users/steve/swarmclaw/package.json:107`). Reuse that pin. ONNX runtime ships transitively via transformers.js. |
| API the story uses | `pipeline('feature-extraction', 'Xenova/all-MiniLM-L6-v2', { dtype: 'fp32' })` then `await pipe(text, { pooling: 'mean', normalize: true })` → `output.data` is a `Float32Array` of **length 384**. |
| Working local example (copy this exactly) | swarmclaw `embeddings.ts:30-34, 68-72`:<br>```js\nconst { pipeline } = await import('@huggingface/transformers');\nconst pipe = await pipeline('feature-extraction', 'Xenova/all-MiniLM-L6-v2', { dtype: 'fp32' });\nconst output = await pipe(text.slice(0, 8000), { pooling: 'mean', normalize: true });\nreturn Array.from(output.data); // 384 floats, L2-normalized\n``` Serialize for SQLite as `Buffer.from(new Float32Array(embedding).buffer)` and read back with `new Float32Array(buf.buffer, buf.byteOffset, buf.byteLength/4)` (swarmclaw `embeddings.ts:122-128`). |
| INV-OM4 (no remote fetch) | swarmclaw's code does **not** set offline flags, so it WILL hit the HF CDN on first run. For QDS-MEM-003 you must enforce local-only:<br>```js\nimport { env } from '@huggingface/transformers';\nenv.allowRemoteModels = false;\nenv.localModelPath = '/abs/path/to/models'; // pinned vendored dir\n// optionally: env.backends.onnx.wasm.wasmPaths = '/abs/path/to/wasm/';\n``` With `allowRemoteModels=false`, the lib loads only from `localModelPath`/cache. This is the concrete mechanism that satisfies **INV-OM4 (pinned local path only, no remote fetch)** and the P2.3 DoD "zero LLM/HTTP egress (network mock asserts)". |
| Determinism (P2.3 DoD: "ONNX deterministic 384-dim") | Use `dtype: 'fp32'` (full precision, as swarmclaw does) — **not** the default quantized model — to get stable, reproducible vectors. `pooling:'mean', normalize:true` are required for the 384-vector to match the model's intended sentence embedding. |
| Gotchas | (1) Model name stays `Xenova/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` even under the `@huggingface/transformers` scope. (2) First load is slow (model init); swarmclaw caches the pipeline in an HMR singleton (`embeddings.ts:13-16`) — replicate a lazy singleton. (3) Vendoring the model files into the repo/image is required for INV-OM4 and for the network-mock test to pass; the model is ~23MB fp32. (4) `dtype:'fp32'` vs quantized changes vector values — pick one and pin it, or the deterministic-output test will flake across environments. |

Source: Context7 `/huggingface/transformers.js` (README env vars, `tutorials/node.md`, `pipelines.md`) + swarmclaw `src/lib/server/embeddings.ts`.

### A.3 bun:sqlite / better-sqlite3 (transactions, prepared stmts, FTS5) — P2.1/P2.2

| Field | Fact |
|---|---|
| Package / version | pi-mem substrate uses **`bun:sqlite`** (built into Bun, no version pin). The local reference impl (swarmclaw) uses **`better-sqlite3 ^12.6.2`** + `@types/better-sqlite3 ^7.6.13` (`package.json:135,122`). Both expose the same sync API shape (`new Database()`, `.prepare()`, `.exec()`, `.pragma()`, `.transaction()`), which is why pi-mem is "Bun/Node dual." Plan calls for dual-runtime wiring at P2.0 (L132). |
| API the story uses | `db.pragma('journal_mode = WAL')`, `db.pragma('busy_timeout = 5000')`; prepared statements via `db.prepare(sql)` reused across calls; **atomic multi-table writes via `db.transaction(fn)`** — exactly the "one tool call → one `events` + one `event_vec` row, transactional" requirement of P2.3 (plan L135). |
| Working local example | swarmclaw `memory-db.ts`: WAL+busy_timeout setup (`:521-523`), FTS5 virtual table + sync triggers (`:567-607`), prepared-statement object (`:670-759`), and `db.transaction(() => {…})()` for atomic link updates (`:1034-1047`, `:627-647`). The FTS5 declaration to copy: ```sql\nCREATE VIRTUAL TABLE memories_fts USING fts5(\n  title, content, category,\n  content='memories', content_rowid='rowid'\n);``` with AFTER INSERT/DELETE/UPDATE triggers keeping it in sync. |
| Gotchas | (1) `better-sqlite3` is **synchronous** — embedding computation must stay off the write path; swarmclaw inserts the row first then fills `embedding` in a fire-and-forget `.then()` (`memory-db.ts:862-870`). For P2.3's "transactional event+vector" you instead want them in **one** `db.transaction`, so compute the embedding *before* opening the transaction. (2) FTS5 `MATCH` syntax is strict — swarmclaw sanitizes/quotes terms and caps term count (`buildFtsQuery`, `:372-396`) to avoid syntax errors from raw user text. (3) `bun:sqlite` loads native extensions (sqlite-vec) differently from `better-sqlite3` — the dual-runtime extension-load path is the SPIKE-11 risk. (4) WAL mode + `busy_timeout` are needed for the nightly cron (P2.7) reading while capture writes. |

Source: swarmclaw `src/lib/server/memory/memory-db.ts`; `package.json`; plan L31, L132, L135.

### A.4 Scheduler / cron — P2.7 (nightly promotion), P2.11 (confirm cron)

| Field | Fact |
|---|---|
| What's needed | Two distinct cadences: **nightly promotion cron** (P2.7, off-peak daily, distills day's events → "Daily Learnings" → `retain()`) and **30-day confirm cron** (P2.11, post-deploy regression check + auto-rollback). Design L277-289 explicitly says "A single scheduled cron runs once per night (off-peak)", skips idle nights below a volume threshold, caps doc size, "is tunable." |
| Library choice — NOT yet pinned | The plan/design specify **behavior** but do **not** name a cron library. swarmclaw already depends on **`cron-parser ^5.5.0`** and **`cronstrue ^3.12.0`** (`package.json:141-142`) — these *parse/describe* cron expressions but do **not** schedule. swarmclaw's own scheduling is a custom runtime loop (`src/lib/server/runtime/scheduler.ts`), not `node-cron`. **Recommendation for writers:** treat the scheduler as a small **decision the P2.7 story must make explicit** — options: (a) `node-cron` (in-process, simplest, matches the "single scheduled cron" framing); (b) `croner` (zero-dep, TS-native, Bun-friendly — better dual-runtime fit than node-cron); (c) reuse swarmclaw's existing `scheduler.ts` runtime loop + `cron-parser` for next-fire computation (consistency with the host). Given Bun/Node dual-runtime (INV) and that `node-cron`'s timer semantics are plain `setInterval`-style, **`croner` or the existing `cron-parser`+custom-loop are the lower-risk picks**; `node-cron` is acceptable but verify Bun compatibility. |
| Example (croner) | ```ts\nimport { Cron } from "croner";\nnew Cron("0 3 * * *", { timezone: "America/New_York" }, async () => {\n  if (await belowVolumeThreshold()) return; // skip idle nights\n  await runNightlyPromotion();\n});\n``` |
| Gotchas | (1) Cron must be **idempotent / single-instance** — a double-fire would double-`retain()`; gate on a run-marker row. (2) Idle-night skip + doc-size cap are explicit requirements (design L286). (3) Disambiguation the writers MUST preserve: this **operational nightly cron ≠ Hindsight strategic cron agents** (Synthesizer/Meta-Optimizer/Blind-Spot, weekly/biweekly/monthly, which are Hindsight `CronCreate` entries per master-design L279). Different tiers (plan L226, design L288-289). (4) P2.11 confirm cron needs durable `confirm_until=+30d` state and a baseline snapshot to compare against. |

Source: plan L97, L144, L153, L226; design L22-23, L277-289, L326-335; swarmclaw `package.json:141-142`, `runtime/scheduler.ts`.

## PART B — Integration Seam Report

### B.1 pi-mono ExtensionAPI — the FREE capture seam (D4) — grounds P2.3 / P2.5 / P2.6

**Status: FULLY GROUNDED (real source read).** Located at `/Users/steve/QDS-Factory-Old/pi-mono/packages/coding-agent/`. Type definitions: `src/core/extensions/types.ts`. Docs: `docs/extensions.md`. Package: `@mariozechner/pi-coding-agent`.

**How an extension subscribes** (`docs/extensions.md:55-97`): an extension is a TS module exporting a default function that receives `pi: ExtensionAPI` and calls `pi.on(eventName, async (event, ctx) => {...})`. Auto-discovered from `~/.pi/agent/extensions/*.ts` (global) or `.pi/extensions/*.ts` (project-local). Hot-reloadable via `/reload`. This is the "fresh-but-thin capture adapter on the free seam" the plan describes (L20, L40, L68).

**The four event payload shapes the capture stories wire into** (exact, from `types.ts`):

```ts
// tool_execution_start  (types.ts:542-547)   -> P2.3 tool-call capture
{ type: "tool_execution_start"; toolCallId: string; toolName: string; args: any }

// tool_execution_update (types.ts:550-556)
{ type: "tool_execution_update"; toolCallId: string; toolName: string; args: any; partialResult: any }

// tool_execution_end    (types.ts:559-565)   -> P2.3 (result + isError)
{ type: "tool_execution_end"; toolCallId: string; toolName: string; result: any; isError: boolean }

// turn_start (types.ts:508-512) / turn_end (types.ts:515-520)  -> P2.5 turn capture
{ type: "turn_start"; turnIndex: number; timestamp: number }
{ type: "turn_end";   turnIndex: number; message: AgentMessage; toolResults: ToolResultMessage[] }

// message_update (types.ts:529-533)  -> streaming, P2.6
{ type: "message_update"; message: AgentMessage; assistantMessageEvent: AssistantMessageEvent }

// agent_end (types.ts:502-505)  -> end-of-prompt boundary, P2.6
{ type: "agent_end"; messages: AgentMessage[] }
```

Subscription is fully typed: `pi.on("tool_execution_end", handler)`, `pi.on("turn_end", …)`, etc. (overloads at `types.ts:941-949`). Lifecycle order (doc diagram L227-258): `agent_start → [turn_start → tool_call → tool_execution_start → tool_execution_update → tool_execution_end → tool_result → turn_end]* → agent_end`. The handler `ctx` (`ExtensionContext`) gives read-only `ctx.sessionManager`, `ctx.cwd`, `ctx.model` — enough to tag captured events with session/model without any LLM call.

**Why this matters for P2.3 DoD:** these events carry `toolName`, `args`, `result`, `isError` synchronously and locally — zero LLM, zero HTTP. The adapter's only job is mechanical: on `tool_execution_end`, embed (ONNX, A.2) + write one `events` row + one `event_vec` row in one `bun:sqlite` transaction (A.3). That satisfies "zero LLM/HTTP egress; p99 < 5ms; deterministic 384-dim."

### B.2 swarmclaw DAG executor hook surface (D5) — SPIKE-10, shape declared UNKNOWN by design — grounds P2.4 / P2.10

**Status: PARTIALLY GROUNDED — and this confirms exactly why SPIKE-10 exists.** Explored `/Users/steve/swarmclaw`. There is a rich DAG/protocol engine at `src/lib/server/protocols/` (`protocol-run-lifecycle.ts`, `protocol-step-processors.ts`, `protocol-agent-turn.ts`, `protocol-swarm.ts`, `protocol-foreach.ts`, etc.) and a typed event model — but **NO programmatic in-process hook/emitter/subscribe surface a capture adapter can attach to.** Findings:

1. **A typed run-event vocabulary EXISTS** (`src/types/protocol.ts:387-443`, `ProtocolRunEvent`). The 30+ event `type`s are exactly the node transitions P2.4 needs: `phase_started`, `phase_completed`, `step_started`, `step_completed`, `step_ready`, `step_waiting`, `step_failed`, `branch_taken`, `parallel_branch_spawned`, `parallel_branch_completed`, `loop_iteration_started/completed`, `subflow_started/completed`, `swarm_opened/claimed/exhausted`, `task_dispatched`, `delegation_dispatched`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`. Each event carries `{ id, runId, type, summary, phaseId?, stepId?, agentId?, taskId?, createdAt, data? }`. **These ARE the D5 "node transitions + handoffs" — the event tree via `parent_event_id` (plan L136) maps naturally onto `runId`+`stepId`.**

2. **Handoff packets EXIST** as a **pull/HTTP** surface, not a push hook: `GET /api/runs/[id]/handoff` and `/api/tasks/[id]/handoff` build a `RunHandoffPacket` (`buildRunHandoffPacket` / `formatRunHandoffMarkdown`, route at `src/app/api/runs/[id]/handoff/route.ts`). And `listUnifiedRunEvents(runId, limit)` (`src/lib/server/runs/unified-run-queries.ts:78`) reads events back.

3. **Persistence is via a repository, not an event bus:** run events are written through `upsertProtocolRunEvent` / `protocolRunEventRepository` (`src/lib/server/protocols/protocol-run-repository.ts:44-80`) — a record store, **no observer/callback registration, no `EventEmitter`, no webhook fan-out** at the executor. The only broadcast primitive found is `notifyOrchestrators(text, contextKey)` (`runtime/orchestrator-events.ts`), which pushes *text* to orchestrator-enabled agents — a coordination nudge, not a structured capture feed.

**What is UNKNOWN / SPIKE-10 must resolve:**
- There is no exposed `onRunEvent(cb)` / subscribe API. P2.4 must choose a tap mechanism, none of which is confirmed yet: **(a) polling** `listUnifiedRunEvents(runId)` (events already persisted, but pull-based, latency + dedup burden); **(b) patching** the executor to call a capture callback at the same site that calls `upsertProtocolRunEvent`; **(c)** consuming the `handoff` HTTP packet at run boundaries (coarse-grained, misses per-step transitions). Plan L95/L136 explicitly tag the hook surface as "shape unknown."
- **Variant-tagged DAGs for A/B (P2.10):** the `ProtocolRun`/`ProtocolSwarmConfig` types do not show a first-class `variant` field. Whether A/B variant tagging rides on `ProtocolRun.data`, on swarm config, or needs a schema add is **unknown → SPIKE-10** (plan L152: "E2E gated on swarmclaw variant-tagged DAGs").
- Whether the executor runs in-process with the capture layer (enabling option b) or out-of-process (forcing a/c) is **unconfirmed**.

**Net for writers:** P2.4 and the E2E half of P2.10 stay **spike-gated on SPIKE-10**; ship interface + unit tests against the *known* `ProtocolRunEvent` shape first (plan L152), wire the real tap after SPIKE-10 picks the mechanism. The good news the writers can rely on: the **event type vocabulary and `{runId, stepId, agentId, type, data}` payload shape are real and stable** — only the *delivery mechanism* is open.

### B.3 Hindsight `retain()` (P2.8 → cross-epic dep P1.11) — grounds the Hindsight bridge

**Status: FULLY GROUNDED.** Signature from `/Users/steve/.agents/skills/hindsight-docs/references/developer/api/retain.md`; bank/tag conventions from `/Users/steve/QDS-Factory/docs/planning/hindsight-memory-master-design.md` (QDS-MEM-001).

**Node.js signature** (`retain.md:30-31, 87-92, 156-160`):
```js
await client.retain(bankId, content, {
  context?:    string,   // source label, shapes fact extraction
  timestamp?:  string,   // ISO 8601 | "unset" | omit→now
  documentId?: string,   // KEY to idempotency — upsert/dedup
  updateMode?: "replace" | "append",   // replace=default upsert; append=concat+reprocess
  metadata?:   Record<string,string>,  // stored on each memory, returned on recall
  entities?:   { text: string; type?: string }[],
  tags?:       string[],          // VISIBILITY SCOPING (intersection at recall)
  // observationScopes per design = "per_tag"
});
```
Python equivalent: `client.retain(bank_id=…, content=…, context=…, timestamp=…, document_id=…)`.

**Banks** (master-design §3): exactly **two** — `qds-product` (§3.1, L78-90: code knowledge — architectural decisions, bug patterns, charter, antipatterns) and `qds-meta` (§3.2, L159-171: factory process — skill outcomes, prompt effectiveness, review catch rates). `observation_scopes: per_tag` on **both** (L72, L90, L171).

**Tag format** — the exact convention the P2.8 bridge must emit (master-design L154, L225-227):
- **qds-product:** `retain(session_summary, tags=[module:X, knowledge_type:Y, severity:Z])` — e.g. `["module:auth", "knowledge_type:antipattern", "severity:high"]`. Value vocab: `knowledge_type:{charter|antipattern|decision}`, `severity:{critical|high|…}`.
- **qds-meta:** `retain(skill_result, tags=[skill:X, outcome:Y, quality_dim:Z])` — e.g. `["skill:synthesizer", "outcome:insight"]`; outcomes seen: `{failure|missed|partial|insight|gap|confirmed|rolled_back}`. P2.11's confirm cron retains a qds-meta finding tagged `outcome:confirmed` or `outcome:rolled_back` (plan L153).

**Critical bridge facts for P2.8:**
1. **Idempotency = `documentId`** (`retain.md:180-186`): pass a stable `documentId` (e.g. `daily-learnings-2026-06-19`) so the nightly cron can safely re-run — Hindsight deletes the old document + its memories and reprocesses. **Omitting `documentId` creates duplicates** on every retry. This is the single most important gotcha for a cron-driven bridge.
2. **`updateMode:"append"`** (`retain.md:188-211`) suits incrementally-growing daily docs (delta-skips unchanged chunks); `"replace"` (default) suits full-doc rewrites. The "Daily Learnings" doc is rewritten once per night, so `replace` + dated `documentId` is the clean model.
3. **No multi-bank retain** — qds-product and qds-meta are separate `retain` calls (master-design L71: "Hindsight does not support multi-bank queries in a single call"). The bridge loops per bank.
4. `tags` are visibility scoping (intersection at recall, `retain.md:219-225`) — they are the partition mechanism, so emitting them correctly is what makes the downstream Synthesizer/Meta-Optimizer queries (P1.11 / master-design §5) actually resolve.

## Epic Summaries (for epics-index insertion)

**P2.7 — Nightly Promotion Cron (4pt, net-new):** Single off-peak nightly job reads the prior day's events over a bounded window (scales with days not events), does LIGHT mechanical consolidation (dedupe, cluster by run/skill/topic, one summary per cluster — deep synthesis stays in Hindsight weekly), and emits one capped, secret-masked "Daily Learnings YYYY-MM-DD" doc (JSON+prose via reused claude-mem ContextBuilder). This is D1: the only LLM in the tier, replacing the stripped per-event SDKAgent. Idle nights below threshold are skipped; idempotency via a `promotion_runs` single-instance run-marker. Cron lib left as an explicit decision (croner recommended; cron-parser+custom loop or Bun-checked node-cron as alternatives). Disambiguated from Hindsight strategic cron agents. Stops at producing the doc — the retain push is P2.8. Deps: P2.3+P2.5+P2.6.

**P2.8 — Hindsight Retain Bridge (3pt, net-new):** Routes the P2.7 Daily Learnings doc per QDS-MEM-001 §6.2 precedence and loops `retain()` once per bank (real §D.5 signature, no multi-bank retain) into qds-product (`module/knowledge_type/severity`) and qds-meta (`skill/outcome/quality_dim`), reusing the cross-epic P1.11 MCP-only client. The load-bearing gotcha: stable dated `documentId` (`daily-learnings-YYYY-MM-DD`) + `updateMode:"replace"` — made the make-or-break AC and INV-P28-1 — so cron retries overwrite instead of duplicating. Resolves SPIKE-9 (D7): batch cron retain, not a live hook. Deps: P2.7 + P1.11 (cross-epic).

## Epic-summaries (for epics-index insertion)

**P2.9 — Propose A/B Experiment and Enforce the One Human Approval Gate (3pt, net-new)**
Wires the front of the A/B lifecycle: optimizer scans recent runs → writes one `proposed` `experiments` row (skill/hypothesis/variant_a_ref/variant_b_ref/metric∈{success_rate|token_cost|duration|review_findings}); CLI/UI approve/reject is THE single human gate (§8.2 — second gate at deploy rejected) flipping `proposed→approved|rejected` with `approved_at`/`approved_by` recorded. Every transition is an audit `events` row written in the same transaction as the flip. No auto-approve path. Depends P2.8.

**P2.10 — Execute Variant-Tagged A/B Runs, Declare Winner, and Deploy with Baseline Snapshot (5pt, net-new) [BLOCKED-BY SPIKE-10 for E2E]**
Wires the middle of the lifecycle: from `approved`, dispatch N variant-tagged swarmclaw runs each (runs/events carry `variant`+`experiment_id`), aggregate metric → `winner∈{a,b,none}` (`running→analyzed`), deploy winner with `deploy_ref` + `baseline_metrics_json` snapshot + `confirm_until=deployed_at+30d` (`analyzed→deployed`). Only `approved` experiments execute; baseline persists before the deploy flip (P2.11 rollback precondition); variant config provenance recorded. Built interface-first against the KNOWN `ProtocolRunEvent` shape; whether `variant` is first-class or rides `ProtocolRun.data` is owned by SPIKE-10. Depends P2.9 + SPIKE-10.

# ADVERSARIAL REVIEW — P2.2 & P2.12

Method: bmad-review-adversarial-general. Posture: cynical, assume sloppy author, hunt structural defects. Findings cite story:line.

---

## P2.2 — Schema Migration Runner

**P2.2:18,34,75 | HIGH | Concurrency on dual-process startup is entirely unaddressed — the canonical migration-runner failure mode.** The story repeatedly says "applied at startup" and "any process opening the operational-memory database brings it to the current schema version" (line 24), and §B research notes the store is dual-runtime (Bun + Node) potentially running simultaneously. Two processes invoking `runMigrations(db)` concurrently on the same SQLite file will race: both read max-applied ordinal, both attempt migration N+1, one fails or double-applies, and INV-MIG3's per-migration transaction does NOT cover the read-then-write ledger gap (TOCTOU). No AC, no invariant, no guard. **Fix:** Add INV-MIG4 (single-migrator concurrency: acquire a SQLite-level lock — `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`/advisory `schema_meta` lock-row, or `PRAGMA locking_mode=EXCLUSIVE` for the migration span) so the ledger read + apply + write is one serialized critical section; add an AC: "Given two processes call `runMigrations` concurrently on a fresh file, Then exactly one applies each migration, the ledger has no duplicate rows, and the loser observes a fully-migrated DB or blocks until it is."

**P2.2:42-50,52,228 | HIGH | The ledger is stored as rows IN `schema_meta` (key/value) but migrations themselves CREATE/seed `schema_meta` — bootstrap circularity is unspecified.** Line 49 says ledger keys live in `schema_meta` (`migration:0001 -> ...`), line 62 makes `0001_init_schema_meta.sql` the migration that CREATES the table. So the runner must write `migration:0001` into a table that migration 0001 is itself creating. Order of operations (create table → run migration body → write ledger row, all within which transaction?) is never stated. If the ledger write is outside 0001's transaction, INV-MIG3 is violated for 0001 specifically; if inside, the runner needs special-casing for the bootstrap migration. **Fix:** Specify the bootstrap sequence explicitly: runner first ensures `schema_meta` exists (idempotent `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` outside the ledgered set, or migration 0001 writes its own ledger row inside its own transaction as the last statement). Add an AC asserting 0001's ledger row is present and consistent after a crash mid-0001.

**P2.2:36,84,36 | HIGH | `schema_version` source of truth is contradictory — seeded constant vs. derived-from-highest-migration.** Line 84/22 seed `schema_version=1.0.0` as a fixed row. Line 36 (AC5) says "`schema_version` equals the highest applied migration's declared version." Migration records carry a `version` field (line 51). These are two different mechanisms. If a future migration 0005 declares version `1.1.0`, does AC5 require `schema_version` to become `1.1.0`, contradicting the fixed seed `1.0.0`? The relationship between per-migration `version` and the single `schema_meta.schema_version` row is undefined. **Fix:** Pick one. Either `schema_version` is a derived value updated by each migration to its declared version (drop it from the static seed), or it is a static product-schema version decoupled from migration ordinals (then delete AC5's "equals highest applied migration's declared version" clause and the per-migration `version` field's role in it).

**P2.2:51,49 | MEDIUM | Ordinal/version/filename triplet has no enforced consistency rule — drift between `0001` filename, `ordinal:1`, and `version` is unguarded.** The in-memory record is `{ordinal, version, filename, sha256}` and the ledger key is `migration:0001`. Nothing asserts the filename's numeric prefix equals `ordinal`, or that ordinals are gap-free/contiguous. A file `0003_...` with no `0002_...` present would let the runner skip silently or misorder. INV-MIG1 only checks "ordinal ≤ max-applied," not contiguity or filename↔ordinal agreement. **Fix:** Add a validation guard: filename prefix must parse to `ordinal`; ordinals must be contiguous from 1 (or explicitly document that gaps are permitted and why); reject the set otherwise. Add to AC4 or a new AC.

**P2.2:74,88 | MEDIUM | INV-MIG2 tamper-evidence is self-defeating: the hash is stored in the same mutable table an attacker editing migrations would also control.** The sha256 of each applied file is stored in `schema_meta`, a plain writable table. An actor who edits an applied `.sql` file can equally update the stored hash row — there is no out-of-band anchor (signed manifest, append-only chain, external attestation). This is "tamper-detection against accidental edits," not tamper-evidence against an adversary, yet INV-MIG2 is filed under Security Invariants claiming the latter. **Fix:** Either downgrade the claim ("detects accidental post-apply edits to migration files") or add a real chain (each ledger row includes hash of previous row → a hash chain rooted at a value not trivially recomputable), and state the threat model.

**P2.2:53,82,18 | MEDIUM | "Convert claude-mem `ensureX()` steps into `.sql` files" — the migration CONTENT is in this story's Files-to-Modify, but P2.1 is declared owner of schema content (line 18,24).** Lines 62-65 list `0002_events_runs_experiments.sql`, `0003_event_edges_event_vec.sql` as files THIS story creates — i.e. the events/runs DDL. But line 18 says "P2.1 owns the schema content (the DDL of events/runs/experiments/event_edges/event_vec); this story owns the mechanism." Direct ownership collision: the DDL physically lives in P2.2's migration files yet P2.1 claims to own it. Two stories will author the same DDL, or one will stub and break the "no stub" guarantee. **Fix:** Decide ownership concretely: either P2.2's `.sql` files contain ONLY `schema_meta` + ledger mechanics and P2.1's store-factory supplies the events/runs DDL as migration content P2.2 consumes; or P2.2 owns all DDL files and P2.1's "schema content" claim is removed. Resolve GAP #1 (FTS5 triggers, owned by P2.1 per §C) and GAP #2 (FK/CASCADE decision, P2.1/P2.2) location too — neither appears in P2.2's migration files despite being DDL.

**P2.2:8,83,192 | MEDIUM | GAP #3 reconciliation is asserted but the migration FROM an existing claude-mem `schema_versions` DB is not handled.** The story standardizes on `schema_meta` and says "the integer table is not vendored" (line 83). But this is a *vendored* substrate (P2.0 copies claude-mem). If any seeded/test DB or substrate artifact already carries `schema_versions`, the runner encounters an existing integer-versioned DB with no `schema_meta`. The story only covers "fresh empty database" (line 22,32). The reconcile is presented as a naming choice, not a data-migration path. **Fix:** State explicitly that this is greenfield (no pre-existing `schema_versions` DBs ever reach this runner) OR add an AC for the upgrade path: existing `schema_versions` DB → backfill `schema_meta` ledger. Given research line 192 frames it as P2.2 reconciling, the greenfield assumption must be written down, not implied.

**P2.2:35,73 | LOW | AC4 / INV-MIG1 conflate two distinct violations (down-migration vs. backfill insert) under one error.** "ordinal ≤ max-applied" rejects both a destructive down-migration and a legitimate-but-late backfill file. The error message and AC don't distinguish, and there's no escape hatch for the (rare, legitimate) need to insert a corrective migration. Acceptable for append-only purity but should be a conscious, stated decision. **Fix:** One sentence affirming "no backfill mechanism exists by design; corrective changes ship as new higher-ordinal migrations."

**P2.2:34 | LOW | AC3 "process surfaces a fatal error" is not binary/testable as written for a non-make-or-break AC — "fatal" is unspecified (throw? exit code? log?).** **Fix:** "throws `MigrationError` and does not return a usable db handle" or "process exits non-zero."

**P2.2:90 | LOW | DoD unit-test box bundles three distinct assertions (idempotency + byte-identical + rollback) into one checkbox, reducing traceability.** Minor; split for signal.

---

## P2.12 — Replay + Retention/Decay + Observability

**P2.12:13,22,34,55,75 | HIGH | The "reversible window" is named as the data-loss safeguard but is never actually reversible — deletion is hard `DELETE`, so the window only *delays* loss, it does not make it reversible.** Lines 13 ("reversible decay policy"), 75 (INV-RPL2 "deletes... reversibly"), and 34/55 all hinge on a "reversible window." But the mechanism (line 22,34,83) is `runRetention()` DELETES events outside the window. Once deleted, an event is gone — there is no tombstone, soft-delete, archive, or restore path. The window is a *grace period before irreversible deletion*, not reversibility. The story's central data-loss safety claim is mislabeled and structurally false. **Fix:** Either rename to "grace window / retention floor" and drop all "reversible" language (honest), OR implement actual reversibility (soft-delete flag, or archive-to-cold-table before delete, with a documented restore path) and add an AC proving an in-window-then-aged event can be recovered. The checklist explicitly asks "is retention bounded so it cannot silently delete recent runs" — the floor addresses recent, but "reversible" is a false promise.

**P2.12:34,55,75,330 | HIGH | Decay deletes `events` rows but the interaction with `event_vec` (vec0 virtual table) and `event_edges` referential rows is unspecified — orphan vectors / dangling edges guaranteed.** AC3/DoD delete events "older than cutoff." §C schema has `event_vec(event_id PK, embedding)` and `event_edges(source_id, target_id)` keyed on event ids. Research line 191 (GAP #2) flags that there are NO FK declarations — so deleting an `events` row does NOT cascade to `event_vec` or `event_edges`. `event_vec` is a sqlite-vec virtual table that does not honor SQL FKs at all. Retention will leave orphaned embedding rows (unbounded vec growth — defeating the entire "bounds growth" purpose, line 14) and dangling edges (corrupting future replays/recall that join them). INV-RPL2 says "bounds growth" but only counts `events`. **Fix:** AC and DoD must require `runRetention()` to delete the matching `event_vec` and `event_edges` rows in the same transaction (explicit deletes, since vec0 won't cascade); add an AC asserting zero orphaned `event_vec`/`event_edges` rows remain after decay; reconcile with GAP #2 FK decision (cite which P2.1/P2.2 decision governs).

**P2.12:22,34,55,83 | HIGH | "Deletes events outside the reversible window" can still delete an in-flight / actively-running run — the decay predicate is age-of-event, not run-status.** The cutoff is `created_at` age (line 22,34). A long-running DAG run started > cutoff ago but still `running` (research §C `runs.status='running'`) has old events that fall outside the window and get deleted mid-run, destroying a live run's history. The checklist explicitly asks "cannot silently delete in-flight or recent runs." Recent is covered by the window floor; in-flight (old-but-active) is NOT. **Fix:** Decay predicate must exclude events whose `run_id` has `runs.status IN ('running')` (and arguably any run with activity inside the window). Add INV-RPL2 clause and an AC: "Given a `running` run with events older than cutoff, When `runRetention()` executes, Then none of that run's events are deleted."

**P2.12:32,55,82 | MEDIUM | <200ms target is asserted but not made deterministically testable — no fixed dataset size / hardware / warm-vs-cold cache baseline.** AC1 requires "≥50 events, ≥3 levels" with "measured wall-clock latency < 200ms," and DoD repeats it. 50 events is trivially small; the real risk is large runs. A 50-event assertion will pass on any machine and proves nothing about the §12.1 target at scale, while a wall-clock assertion is flaky under CI load (GC, cold SQLite page cache). The target is simultaneously too-weak (tiny N) and flaky (wall-clock). **Fix:** Specify a representative N (e.g. ≥1000 events for the perf assertion, separate from the 50-event correctness case), warm the cache, and either assert against a generous CI-safe ceiling with the 200ms target documented as the production goal, or measure on a pinned bench. State the index assumption (the `created_at` + `parent` indexes from §C must exist — depends on P2.1).

**P2.12:35,55,74,87 | MEDIUM | Read-only proof by "row-count + checksum before/after" is insufficient — replay's own observability writes `lastReplayMs`/`lastDecayAt`, and the proof scope vs. the actual write is muddled.** INV-RPL1 (line 74) claims "zero mutations against `events`/`event_edges`/`event_vec`" and the proof is row-count+checksum on those tables. But `getStoreObservability` returns `lastReplayMs`/`lastDecayAt` (line 50-51) — these are state that must be stored *somewhere*. If they live in `schema_meta` or a metrics table, then observability DOES write, and the "no write" framing (DoD line 84 "all via reads only," line 89) is contradicted: you cannot record "last replay latency" via reads only. Where is `lastReplayMs` persisted? **Fix:** Clarify: either these are in-memory process counters (then they reset per process and "last-decay timestamp" survives restart only if persisted — contradiction), or they persist to a metrics row (then state that observability writes ONLY to that metrics row, never to event tables, and scope INV-RPL1's checksum to event tables only, which it sort of does — but DoD line 84 "via reads only" is then wrong). Resolve the persistence location of decay/replay metrics.

**P2.12:55,82,90 | MEDIUM | Cycle-safe tree assembly is required but the malformed-input source is contradictory — capture-side guarantees parent integrity, yet replay must defend against cycles.** Line 55/82 require defending against a "malformed `parent_event_id` cycle." But `parent_event_id` is set at capture (P2.3-P2.6) pointing to an earlier event; a cycle implies event A's parent is a later event B whose parent is A — impossible if parents are always pre-existing earlier events. Either the capture invariant guarantees acyclicity (then cycle-defense is dead code with an untestable AC) or it does NOT (then there's a missing capture-side invariant). The story can't both rely on the capture guarantee (INV-OM2 spine) and assume it's violated. **Fix:** State the threat model: if defending against DB corruption / future writers, say so and keep the visited-set guard with a test that injects a synthetic cycle. Also handle the *dangling parent* case explicitly (line 82 mentions it but no AC) — an event whose `parent_event_id` references a now-decayed/deleted parent (directly created by P2.12's own retention!). This is the realistic orphan, not a cycle.

**P2.12:34,46,55 | MEDIUM | Retention has no dry-run / safety bound on bulk delete and no AC for catastrophic-config protection.** `runRetention()` takes a tunable cutoff (line 55 "tunable config"). A misconfigured cutoff (e.g. cutoff = now) would delete everything outside the window in one transaction. There's no max-delete cap, no dry-run mode, no guard against a cutoff that would delete more than X% of the store. For an "ephemeral but diagnosable" tier, a fat-finger config is a total-history-loss event. **Fix:** Add a guard/limit: max-rows-per-run cap or a sanity bound (refuse if cutoff would delete > N% without an explicit force flag), and an AC for it. At minimum a dry-run that returns the would-delete count (the `RetentionResult.deleted` could double as this with a `dryRun` param).

**P2.12:24,86,330 | LOW | DEFER seam is named (worker HTTP/SSE §B.3 + viewer §13) but the seam's actual SHAPE is not pinned — "data structures without transport" is asserted, yet `ReplayResult` is the only contract; SSE implies streaming/incremental, which a single `ReplayResult` return does not support.** Future SSE consumers may need a cursor/stream, not a materialized full-tree object. **Fix:** One line noting the replay API returns a materialized result and the deferred SSE layer will adapt/paginate it, OR expose an iterator seam now. Low because it's a future concern, but the "seam those future surfaces consume" claim (line 18) is thin.

**P2.12:73,89 | LOW | INV-OM2 (read side) "never fabricates a run for a null/absent spine" is well-stated, but AC2 (empty run) doesn't cover the *non-existent* run_id vs. *existing-but-eventless* run_id distinction.** AC2 says "a run_id with no events." A run_id that was never created at all vs. a created run with zero events may warrant different signals to an optimizer. **Fix:** Clarify both collapse to the same empty result (probably fine) and state it.

## Cross-story / structural

**BOTH:24,24 | LOW | Both stories reuse the "Wiring closed" template near-verbatim per convention, but both claim "no sibling story is required" while P2.12 depends on P2.1's recall primitives AND P2.1's index/schema (the <200ms target is unachievable without P2.1's 9 indexes), and P2.2's `.sql` content overlaps P2.1's DDL ownership.** The "atomic, no sibling needed" framing is technically satisfied via dependency edges but masks the real coupling (P2.12 perf depends on P2.1 indexes existing; P2.2 DDL depends on P2.1 ownership resolution). Already captured in P2.2:53 and P2.12:32 above; noting the pattern.

## P2.6 — Outcome Capture (glue)

`p2-6-outcome-capture.md:48,55 | HIGH | The NULL outcome value is admitted into the enum ({success,partial,failure,skipped,NULL}) but its capture semantics are undefined. An adapter is supposed to record a verdict; what does it mean for an adapter to fire and write outcome=NULL? AC#6 rollup says "≥1 success → success; else NULL" — so NULL is only a rollup-result state, yet the per-event type also permits NULL with no Given/When/Then covering when an adapter emits it. This is an untestable degree of freedom that a dev will resolve arbitrarily. | Either (a) forbid NULL at the per-event adapter level (adapters always emit one of success/partial/failure/skipped) and document NULL strictly as the empty-rollup result, or (b) add an AC defining the precondition under which an adapter writes outcome=NULL. `p2-6-outcome-capture.md:55,59 | HIGH | Rollup precedence is under-specified for the runs.overall_outcome UPDATE timing and its interaction with the idempotency guard. If a step re-fires with a DIFFERENT verdict (flaky test: failure then success on retry), does the second write replace or is it rejected as a duplicate? "Idempotent on retry" silently means the FIRST verdict wins and a real recovery is lost. | Define the (run,source) collision rule explicitly: last-write-wins (UPSERT) vs first-write-wins, and re-run the rollup after any replace. Add an AC for the re-fire-with-changed-verdict path — it is the realistic flaky-test case, not an edge.`

A make-or-break-adjacent guard scenario that accepts two different observable states cannot be asserted deterministically — a dev can satisfy it either way and a reviewer cannot fail it. | Pick one: either a gated-off review MUST emit an outcome='skipped' row (preferred — keeps the run diagnosable, which is the whole G3 rationale), OR it MUST emit no row. `p2-6-outcome-capture.md:18,35,37 | MEDIUM | The any-failure-dominates precedence does not define behavior when a source's outcome is the per-event NULL (not skipped). §55 rollup comment lumps "skipped/NULL non-failing" but DoD §87 test table only enumerates failure/success/partial/skipped — no row exercises a per-event NULL in the rollup. If NULL events can exist (per the enum), the rollup must classify them; the test table doesn't. | Add the per-event-NULL case to the rollup precedence comment AND the DoD unit-test table, or remove per-event NULL per the prior finding so the gap closes.`

The §E.6 brief explicitly lists "external CodeRabbit harvesting (Hindsight Review Harvester owns it §2)" as Out. | Add an explicit "Out of scope" bullet (Technical Notes or Overview) naming: trend aggregation, external CodeRabbit harvesting (Hindsight Review Harvester §2), and LLM classification (P2.7) — so the no-overlap-with-Hindsight invariant is auditable, not implied.`

`p2-6-outcome-capture.md:33 | LOW | AC#2 asserts "no @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk query() is invoked" but provides no mechanism to detect invocation — a network mock asserts zero HTTP, but the SDK could be imported/constructed without egress and still violate INV-OM1's "no SDK path reachable." | State the detection mechanism (e.g. module-import assertion / spy on the SDK entrypoint), matching how P2.3's "no SDK path reachable" is to be enforced.`

## Spike-Soundness Verdict (P2.4)

Genuinely interface-first and largely build-ready: it defines the node event shape, the parent_event_id tree contract, the source-decoupled ingestProtocolRunEvents entry, and correctly defers the live-tap to SPIKE-10. It avoids forbidden ⛔/TBD/pending-sign-off markers while staying honest that E2E is gated. HOWEVER, three contract bugs (node identity collision, root-node parent, handoff target ordering) mean the unit-testable contracts are NOT actually closeable NOW as written — they would close a broken mapper. p2-11-confirm-cron.md:32 | HIGH | The make-or-break AC measures a system-throughput SLA ("≥95% of deployed experiments reach terminal within 35d") that this story's code **cannot satisfy alone**. It depends on P2.10 actually producing `deployed` rows with correct `confirm_until`, on the cron scheduler firing daily in prod, and on a "steady stream of deployed experiments" that does not exist in a unit/E2E test. A single-story make-or-break AC must be binary and verifiable from THIS story's merged code. As written it is an operational/fleet metric requiring 35 real days + upstream production traffic — untestable at merge time, so the gate is unfalsifiable at story acceptance. | Reframe the binary make-or-break to a deterministic property of the cron itself, e.g.: "Given a seeded `deployed` experiment with `confirm_until ≤ now`, When `ConfirmCron.run(now)` executes, Then the row is in `confirmed` or `rolled_back` (never left `deployed` or stuck `confirming`) with `decided_at` set — for 100% of eligible rows in a single tick." Move the ≥95%/35-day fleet number to an epic-level observability metric, not a per-story make-or-break.

p2-11-confirm-cron.md:33,37,63 | HIGH | Fail-safe direction is asserted for *comparison error / insufficient data* (AC6, INV-CONFIRM1) but NOT for the **`confirming` intermediate state on crash**. The state machine is `deployed → confirming → confirmed|rolled_back`. If the process crashes (or is killed by the single-instance guard's host) *after* writing `confirming` but the per-experiment transaction wraps only the terminal write, a row can be stranded in `confirming` forever. Nothing in the spec says a stranded `confirming` row is fail-safe-reverted on the next tick. The cron scan filters on status `deployed` (DoD line 71), so a `confirming` row is never re-picked-up → it silently holds a possibly-regressed deploy, violating fail-safe. | Add an AC + DoD box: the eligible-row scan MUST also pick up `confirming` rows (orphaned from a prior crashed tick) and resolve them fail-safe (assume regression → `rolled_back` + revert) OR make the entire `deployed → confirming → terminal` sequence a single atomic transaction so `confirming` is never durably observable. State which. Cite which transition boundary the run-marker/transaction covers.

p2-11-confirm-cron.md:36,44,64 | HIGH | The idempotency guard is a per-DAY run-marker (`confirm-cron-YYYY-MM-DD`). This is **wrong-grained for crash-mid-confirm**. If the cron processes 50 experiments, writes the day-marker, crashes after experiment #20, the re-run sees the day-marker and short-circuits (AC5: "no transition is re-applied") → experiments #21–50 never get confirmed/rolled-back that day, and if `confirm_until` logic is "today only" they may be missed. Conversely if the marker is written AFTER the loop, a crash mid-loop leaves no marker and re-run **double-processes** #1–20 — and the per-experiment idempotency relies entirely on the `documentId` for `retain()` and on status-no-longer-`deployed` for the DB write, which IS sound, but then the day-marker is redundant/harmful. The two idempotency mechanisms (day-marker vs per-row status check) conflict and the ordering (marker before/after loop) is unspecified. | Specify: idempotency MUST be **per-experiment**, not per-day. The day-marker (if kept) is only an advisory single-instance lock, NOT a correctness gate — drop the AC5 claim that a 2nd same-day run "short-circuits with no transition." Correctness comes from (a) the scan only selecting still-`deployed` rows and (b) the stable per-experiment `documentId`. Re-write AC5 to: "second invocation re-scans, finds the already-terminal rows excluded by the status filter, re-`retain()`s with the same `documentId`+`updateMode:replace` (no duplicate doc), applies zero new transitions." That is genuinely crash-safe and resume-safe; the day-marker version is not.

p2-11-confirm-cron.md:18,26,44 | HIGH | `deploy_ref` revert reads "auto-reverted to its **pre-deploy value**" but P2.10 (cited predecessor, research-context §E P2.10 / §B.2 line 121) only writes `deploy_ref` = the *deployed* ref and `baseline_metrics_json`. **No pre-deploy `deploy_ref` is snapshotted anywhere in the cited schema** (experiments table, lines 161–171, has a single `deploy_ref TEXT` column — no `prev_deploy_ref`). So "revert to pre-deploy value" has no data source. The rollback is unimplementable as specified without either a schema column P2.10 doesn't write or an external source of the prior ref. | Resolve the data-provenance gap explicitly: either (a) require P2.10 to also persist the prior ref (e.g. into `baseline_metrics_json` or a new column) and cite it as a dependency edge — but that's a P2.10 schema change this story can't make; or (b) define "revert" as a concrete operation that doesn't need the old value (e.g. redeploy `variant_a_ref` / the control variant, which IS in the schema, lines 163). Pick one and write the AC against the real available column. As-is, AC2's "deploy_ref auto-reverted to the pre-deploy value" cannot be asserted.

p2-11-confirm-cron.md:32,35,44 | MEDIUM | `confirm_until = deployed_at + 30d` is stated, but the make-or-break window is 35d and AC4 guards "still inside 30-day window." The 5-day slack (30→35) is asserted nowhere as a cron-cadence guarantee. If the cron runs once/day, an experiment crossing `confirm_until` is picked up within ≤24h, so terminal-within-35d is trivially met for any single row — making the ≥95%/35d metric measure *cron uptime*, not cron logic. The metric is testable only as "did the scheduler run." | Either drop the 35d framing in favor of the per-tick binary property (see line 32 finding), or explicitly state the 5-day buffer absorbs N missed cron ticks and assert "an eligible row is resolved within ≤2 cron ticks of `confirm_until`" as the real testable invariant.

p2-11-confirm-cron.md:42,61 | MEDIUM | INV-OM2 requires non-null `run_id` on every transition event, and Integration points (line 42) say the event sets `experiment_id`. But the experiment's confirm/rollback is a **cron action, not a DAG run** — there is no `run_id` for "the experiment's deploy/confirm run." INV-OM2 (line 61) claims `run_id` = "the experiment's deploy/confirm run," but the cron isn't a swarmclaw run and §C `events.run_id` is `NOT NULL FK runs`. What run row does a cron-emitted transition event FK to? | Specify the `run_id` source for cron-emitted events: either synthesize a deterministic cron run_id (e.g. `confirm-cron-<date>`) and insert a matching `runs` row, or document that confirm-transition events use the original deploy run_id from P2.10. Without this, INV-OM2 fails at insert (FK/NOT NULL violation) or the audit event can't be written — directly undermining INV-CONFIRM3 (audited rollback).

p2-11-confirm-cron.md:33,73 | MEDIUM | "No human approval step invoked" is asserted as an AC outcome (AC2) and DoD (line 73: "NO human-approval path in the code"). This is a **negative/absence assertion** — hard to test and easy to regress silently. There is no positive assertion that the rollback is *audited as a discrete event before the revert commits* tied to AC2 (INV-CONFIRM3 exists at line 65 but AC2 itself doesn't assert the audit-event-precedes-commit ordering). | Add to AC2: "...and a `rolled_back` transition event is written in the same transaction as the `deploy_ref` revert (revert is never committed without its audit event)." Make the audit-before-commit ordering a binary assertion in the AC, not only an invariant.

p2-11-confirm-cron.md:41 | MEDIUM | `isRegression` direction table lists `success_rate` (higher-better) and `token_cost`/`duration`/`review_findings` (lower-better), but P2.9's metric enum (research-context line 308) is `{success_rate | token_cost | duration | review_findings}`. The names don't match exactly: spec metric is `token_cost` but `experiments.metric` could carry the raw enum string. More importantly, **no handling for `winner = none`** experiments — can a `winner:none` experiment ever reach `deployed`? If P2.10 deploys only on a clear winner, fine, but the cron should defensively handle/skip a `deployed` row whose comparison is undefined. | Add a guard AC or note: a `deployed` row with an unrecognized `metric` value or undefined comparison direction falls into the fail-safe rollback path (not a crash, not a silent skip). Confirm the metric-name strings match P2.9's enum verbatim.

Better: store the single-instance lock outside the migration ledger entirely.

p2-11-confirm-cron.md:22,44,79 | LOW | The `documentId` format `confirm-<experiment_id>-YYYY-MM-DD` includes the date. If the cron mis-fires across a midnight boundary on a retry (run started 23:59, retry 00:01), the date component differs → two different `documentId`s for the same experiment verdict → **duplicate retain**, defeating the stated idempotency. The terminal verdict for an experiment is date-independent (it happens once). | Drop the date from the per-experiment `documentId`: use `confirm-<experiment_id>` (the verdict is per-experiment, not per-day) with `updateMode:"replace"`. The dated form is correct for the once-nightly *aggregate* doc (P2.8) but wrong for a per-experiment verdict.

p2-11-confirm-cron.md:41,75 | LOW | `baseline_metrics_json` shape `{ metric, value, n, window_ms }` is defined inline here, but P2.10 is the writer and research-context never pins that shape. This story assumes a contract the predecessor spec doesn't declare. | Cite the exact P2.10-owned shape by name/owner ("validated against P2.10 `BaselineMetrics` shape") rather than inventing it here; if P2.10 doesn't define it, flag that P2.10 must, as a dependency-edge contract.

p2-11-confirm-cron.md:53-54,46-54 | LOW | Files-to-Modify lists 6 paths but omits the cron-registration/scheduler wiring file. The story says the cron fires once/day via `croner` (line 42), but no entrypoint/scheduler-registration file is in the modify list — so "fires once/day off-peak" is wired nowhere a reviewer can point to. | Add the scheduler-registration file (where `croner` schedules `ConfirmCron.run`) to Files-to-Modify, or state explicitly that registration is deferred to an integration/bootstrap story and the cron is invoked directly in tests.

---

## Summary

The story is well-structured and the fail-safe *intent* is clearly stated, but four structural defects make the spec unimplementable-as-written or untestable-at-merge:

1. **Make-or-break is a fleet/uptime SLA, not a per-story binary** (line 32).
2. **`confirming` intermediate state has no crash-recovery path** — fail-safe covers comparison errors but not process death mid-transition (lines 33/37/63).
3. **Idempotency is per-day, which is wrong-grained for crash-mid-confirm** and conflicts with the sound per-row status/documentId mechanism (lines 36/44/64).
4. **"Revert deploy_ref to pre-deploy value" has no data source** in the cited P2.10/schema — no prior-ref column exists (lines 18/26/44).

These four must be resolved before development; the MEDIUMs (cron `run_id` FK source, schema_meta boundary, metric-name match, audit-before-commit AC) are correctness gaps that will surface as runtime failures.

# Adversarial Review — P2.3 & P2.5 Capture Story Specs

Method: bmad-review-adversarial-general. Posture: assume the spec is lying until the line proves itself. Cross-checked against research-context.md §B (INV-OM1..4), §C DDL, §D.5 real pi-mono event shapes.

---

## CHECKLIST 1: secrets (INV-OM3 — maskAndCap shared util, mask before write AND before embed)

**P2.3:42 | HIGH | The shared `maskAndCap` masks the *stored* payload, but the embedding is computed from a SEPARATE unmasked text path — secrets WILL leak into `event_vec`.** Line 43 defines embed text as `` `${event_type} ${skill} ${tool_name} ${payload-summary}` `` where `payload-summary` is derived independently of `maskAndCap`. Nothing in the spec asserts the embed input is the masked output. An `sk-...` token in tool args becomes part of the 384-dim vector (and the vector is a lossy-but-recoverable projection; worse, the `payload-summary` substring may be stored verbatim in any debug path). The checklist explicitly requires "masking applied BEFORE write AND BEFORE embedding so secrets don't leak into event_vec" — the spec satisfies the first, silently fails the second. **Fix:** Add to Technical Notes + a DoD box: "embed input text is derived from the `maskAndCap`-masked payload, not raw args — verified by a test embedding a payload containing `sk-test...` and asserting the secret never reaches `embedText()` (spy on the embed input)." Add AC: secret-in-args → secret absent from both `payload_json` AND the embedded text.

**P2.5:43 | HIGH | Same embed-leak defect, and worse: P2.5 embeds raw reasoning/message text where secrets are MORE likely (agents paste keys into chat).** Line 43 embed text = `` `${event_type} ${skill} ${reasoning/message text}` ``. The `reasoning/message text` is the raw `messageText`, while `maskAndCap(messageText)` only protects `payload_json`. Reasoning streams routinely contain pasted credentials, env dumps, and tool outputs. **Fix:** Embed only the masked text; mirror the P2.3 fix with a P2.5-specific test.

**P2.3:42 | MEDIUM | "Generic `key|secret|token|password` k/v" regex is named but not specified — under-defined masking that a dev will implement inconsistently and that P2.5 inherits.** A k/v regex must define delimiter set (`:`, `=`, `":"`), quoting, multiline, and value-capture boundaries. "Apply ordered secret-pattern regex set" with a parenthetical is not a spec; it is a wish. INV-OM3 is the one invariant where "approximately masked" equals "leaked." **Fix:** Either inline the concrete regex set (anchors + flags) in Technical Notes, or cite a fixture file `tests/capture/secret-corpus.json` enumerating each class with positive+negative cases, and require the unit test to drive every class from that fixture.

**P2.5:42 | LOW | Reuse is asserted in prose ("import P2.3 `maskAndCap`... adds no masking code") and well-enforced in DoD:76 — this is correct.** Stated as a strength: P2.5 explicitly forbids re-implementation and has a DoD box "no duplicate masking code." This part of the secrets checklist passes. The only residual risk is the embed path above.

---

## CHECKLIST 2: zero-egress (INV-OM1 — network mock in DoD; P2.3 verbatim constraints)

**P2.3:33 / 81 | LOW | Verbatim constraints present and correct.** AC#1 and DoD final box both carry "network mock... zero outbound calls" + "p99 < 5 ms over 1000 calls"; AC#4/line 43 carry "ONNX deterministic... fp32/mean/normalize/384-dim." This matches §D.5 / §E.1 P2.3 verbatim DoD requirement. Passes.

**P2.3:33 | MEDIUM | The model load is itself a network operation that the network mock will throw on — the spec never sequences mock-install vs. model-warmup, so the make-or-break test is ambiguous about whether the 23MB model is already resident.** §D.4 notes the model is ~23MB and `env.localModelPath` makes it "load local-only (this is how the network-mock test passes)." But if the lazy-singleton pipeline is *first* initialized inside the mocked window, transformers.js may still attempt a remote HEAD/metadata check before falling back, and the test outcome depends on load order. AC#1 says "1000 events dispatched through the adapter" with the mock active — does that include the cold model load? **Fix:** Specify in the e2e DoD: "model pipeline is warm-loaded from `env.localModelPath` BEFORE the network mock is installed; the mock asserts zero outbound calls during *event dispatch*." Otherwise the make-or-break test is non-deterministic on a clean machine.

**P2.5:33 | HIGH | The make-or-break AC for P2.5 dropped the `p99 < 5 ms` and ONNX-determinism performance constraints that P2.3 carries — and P2.5 does MORE per-turn work (buffering + coalescing).** §B/§E.1 binds INV-OM4 on "store/embed (P2.1, P2.3, **P2.5**)." P2.3 AC#1 measures p99 latency; P2.5 AC#1 (line 33) only asserts row counts + zero egress, no latency bound. P2.5's turn-buffer coalescing is non-trivial and is exactly where a slow path hides. **Fix:** Add a latency bound to P2.5 AC#1 (e.g. "p99 < 5 ms per `turn_end` flush over N turns") consistent with the canonical-pattern claim on line 19 ("follows the canonical capture pattern established by P2.3 exactly").

**P2.5:64 | MEDIUM | INV-OM4 stated for P2.5 but `env.localModelPath` is only mentioned as "pinned `env.localModelPath`" (line 43) and the invariant text (line 64) omits it entirely** — unlike P2.3:66 which names both `allowRemoteModels=false` AND `localModelPath`. Since P2.5 "reuses P2.3 embed.ts" (line 43), the local path is inherited, but the invariant as written is weaker than P2.3's. **Fix:** Make INV-OM4 wording identical across both stories, or state "via reused P2.3 `embed.ts` which sets both `allowRemoteModels=false` and `localModelPath`."

---

## CHECKLIST 3: deserialization (args/payloads parsed safely, 64KB-bounded BEFORE persistence)

**P2.3:42 | HIGH | `maskAndCap` JSON-stringifies untrusted tool `result`/`args` with no guard against circular refs, BigInt, or throwing serializers — an exception in capture will either crash the agent or silently drop the event, and the spec specifies neither.** `JSON.stringify` throws on circular structures and on `BigInt`. Tool results are arbitrary (a `bash` tool returning a buffer, a tool returning a value with a `toJSON` that throws). The util is declared "pure, sync, no I/O" with zero error contract. If it throws inside `pi.on("tool_execution_end")`, what happens to the agent? INV-OM2/atomicity say nothing about serialization failure. **Fix:** Specify `maskAndCap` is total (never throws): wrap stringify in try/catch with a circular-safe replacer, return a sentinel `'{"_capture_error":"unserializable"}'` (still ≤64KB) on failure, and add a unit test feeding a circular object + a BigInt. Add a DoD box: "capture handler never throws into the pi-mono event loop (a malformed/unserializable payload yields a sentinel row, not an agent crash)."

**P2.5:42 | HIGH | Same unserializable-payload exposure, inherited via reuse, plus an additional `turn_end.toolResults` shape that the row-mapping (line 41) extracts `messageText` from but never bounds.** Line 41 maps `payload_json:maskAndCap(messageText)` — but `turn_end {turnIndex, message, toolResults}` (§D.5) carries BOTH `message` and `toolResults`, and the mapping silently discards `toolResults` while the embed text uses ambiguous "reasoning/message text." If `message` is itself a structured object (not a string), `maskAndCap(messageText)` where `messageText` is undefined produces `"undefined"` or throws. **Fix:** Define exactly how `messageText` is extracted from `TurnEndEvent.message` (string vs object-with-content-array), specify whether `toolResults` is captured or intentionally dropped (P2.3 already captures tool results — capturing them again here is duplication; dropping them should be explicit), and bound before persistence.

**P2.3:35 | MEDIUM | The 64KB cap is "UTF-8 safe... hard-truncate to 65536 bytes" but AC#3 verifies `byte-length ≤ 65536` AFTER masking — truncating mid-secret could leave a partial unmasked secret if cap runs before mask.** Line 42 specifies order "mask THEN truncate" (correct), but AC#3 line 35 says "exceeds 64KB AND contains a secret" → "≤64KB and secret masked." If a secret sits at byte 70000, mask-then-cap masks it then truncates it away — fine. But if the truncation marker `…[truncated]` is appended AFTER the byte cap, the result exceeds 65536. **Fix:** Specify the truncation reserves marker length: truncate to `65536 - len(marker)` bytes then append marker; assert final byte length ≤ 65536 inclusive of marker.

**P2.5:35 | MEDIUM | AC#3 inherits the same cap-marker arithmetic ambiguity** (mirrors P2.3:35). Same fix.

---

## CHECKLIST 4: threading/correctness (INV-OM2 orphan-event path; §D.5 event shapes; P2.5 buffering losslessness)

**P2.3:45 / P2.5:45 | HIGH (structural, both) | The orphan-event failure path is UNSPECIFIED. INV-OM2 demands `run_id` non-null on every row, but neither spec says what the handler does when `tool_execution_end`/`turn_end` fires with NO active run context.** Line 45 (both) resolves `run_id` "from a capture-session context (env/`ctx.sessionManager`)" and asserts "`run_id` MUST be non-null." But pi-mono fires events; the extension does not control whether a run context exists (e.g. a tool call during agent bootstrap, a session started outside a tracked run, `ctx.sessionManager` returning null). DoD:77 (P2.3) / DoD:75 (P2.5) say "rejects/guards null" — but "reject" means the event is DROPPED (data loss, silent gap in the replayable spine — the entire G-LEARN value prop), while "guard" is undefined. This is the single most important correctness hole: an unspecified branch on the spine's defining invariant. **Fix:** Specify the orphan policy explicitly and add an AC for it. Recommended: synthesize a deterministic fallback `run_id` (e.g. `orphan:${sessionId}` from `ctx.sessionManager`) so the row is still written and never null, OR write to a dead-letter table — but pick one and make it a binary AC: "Given `tool_execution_end` fires with no resolvable run context, When the handler runs, Then [a row with a synthesized non-null run_id is written | the event is recorded to dead-letter] — never a null `run_id` and never a silent drop." Without this, INV-OM2 is enforced by crashing or by losing data.

**P2.5:44 | HIGH | The pi-mono event-name line numbers contradict §D.5 and are internally inconsistent with the row-mapping line — the spec cites THREE different `types.ts` line numbers for the same events.** Line 44 cites `pi.on(... )` at `types.ts:945/943/941`; line 41 cites the payload shapes at `types.ts:515/529/502`; the Overview line 19 cites `types.ts:529/515/502`. §D.5 of research-context gives NO line numbers, only shapes. These fabricated, mutually-inconsistent line citations (`turn_end` is variously :515, :529, :943) will send the dev to the wrong line and erode trust in every other citation. P2.3 has the same smell (line 41 cites `types.ts:559` for `ToolExecutionEndEvent`; line 44 cites `types.ts:949` for `.on` and `:261` for context). **Fix:** Drop precise line numbers OR verify them against the actual `pi-mono/.../types.ts` before merge. Cite the file + symbol name (`ExtensionAPI.on`, `ToolExecutionEndEvent`) which is stable; line numbers are not.

**P2.5:33,37,45,71 | HIGH | The buffering model is NOT lossless and the AC contradicts itself on flush boundaries — `message_update` buffered → one row per `turn_end`, but also "flushed on `agent_end`": if a turn's updates arrive and `agent_end` fires WITHOUT a closing `turn_end`, is that a row or a drop?** §D.5 lifecycle: `agent_start → [turn_start → ... → turn_end]* → agent_end`. AC#1 (line 33) writes "one row per completed turn" on `turn_end`. AC#5 (line 37) + DoD (line 71) write a row on `agent_end` for "any open partial message." But the checklist requires "message_update → one message event per turn_end, flush on agent_end" be lossless. Two losslessness gaps: (a) if `message_update`s accumulate and `agent_end` fires after the last `turn_end` already flushed them, the `agent_end` flush writes an EMPTY/duplicate row — AC#5's idempotency guard ("no duplicate message rows for the same turn") collides with the line-71 "flush any open partial" mandate; (b) if a turn is interrupted (`agent_end` mid-turn, no `turn_end`), the partial must be flushed exactly once. The spec asserts both "no duplicates" and "flush on agent_end" without defining the buffer's empty-check. **Fix:** Specify the turn-buffer state machine precisely: buffer accumulates `message_update` text keyed by current `turnIndex`; `turn_end` flushes+clears the buffer for that turn (one row); `agent_end` flushes ONLY if the buffer is non-empty (interrupted turn), else no-op. Make AC#5 binary on both arms: "agent_end after a clean turn_end → zero additional rows; agent_end with buffered-but-unterminated text → exactly one row." Add a turn-buffer unit test for the interrupted-turn case.

**P2.3:45 | MEDIUM | `parent_event_id` = "the most recent open event in the current turn" is ordering-dependent and racy under concurrency, with no definition of "open" — the event-tree (D2, the replay value) can be silently wrong.** What makes an event "open"? Tool calls in pi-mono `tool_execution_*` can interleave (parallel tool calls per §D.5 `parallel_branch_spawned`). "Most recent" by wall-clock `created_at` is not the same as the causal parent. There is no spec for how the adapter tracks the open-event stack. **Fix:** Define the parent-resolution rule concretely (e.g. parent = the `node`/`turn_start` event for the current `turnIndex`, NOT "most recent open"), and add an AC asserting the parent linkage for a known two-tool-call turn.

**P2.3:34 | MEDIUM | Event shape match is correct (`{toolCallId, toolName, result, isError}`) but the row mapping uses `args` (`payload_json:maskAndCap(args)`) and `duration_ms` — NEITHER is in the §D.5 `tool_execution_end` payload.** §D.5: `tool_execution_end {toolCallId, toolName, result, isError}` — no `args`, no duration. `args` is on `tool_execution_start {toolCallId, toolName, args}`. So `payload_json:maskAndCap(args)` (line 41) references a field the event does not carry; the adapter must correlate `tool_execution_start.args` by `toolCallId` across two events, which the spec never mentions. Same for `duration_ms` (must be computed from start→end timestamps). **Fix:** Specify the start/end correlation: buffer `tool_execution_start` args + timestamp keyed by `toolCallId`, join on `tool_execution_end` to populate `payload_json` and `duration_ms`. Add this to Integration points and a DoD box. As written, `payload_json` would be undefined.

**P2.3:34 / P2.5:34 | MEDIUM | `outcome` enum and `tokens` field are written but not validated against the §C DDL CHECK constraints.** §C: `event_type IN ('tool_call','node','message','outcome')` is CHECK'd; `outcome` has no CHECK in DDL but P2.6 uses `{success,partial,failure,skipped,NULL}`. P2.3 writes `outcome:'success'|'failure'` (line 41) — fine. But P2.5 line 41 row-mapping has NO `outcome` field and NO `result_json`, yet the DDL marks `payload_json TEXT NOT NULL` — confirm `message` events satisfy NOT NULL (they do via payload_json) but `tokens` source is unspecified (`turn_end` payload has no token count per §D.5). **Fix (P2.5):** Specify where `tokens` comes from (`assistantMessageEvent`? omit and default 0 per DDL). Don't write a column you can't source.

**P2.3:51-57 / P2.5:51-55 | MEDIUM | P2.3 declares `src/capture/threading.ts` as a net-new file but P2.5 (which claims identical §5.2 threading, line 45) does NOT list it in Files to Modify and never states it reuses P2.3's threading.ts — threading provenance is ambiguous (re-implemented or shared?).** P2.3 authors `threading.ts`; P2.5 line 45 says "identical to P2.3" but Files-to-Modify (lines 51-55) omits it, implying reuse, yet unlike `maskAndCap` (explicitly "reused, not re-authored") threading reuse is never asserted. A dev could re-implement threading in `pi-reasoning-extension.ts`, drifting the two adapters' run_id resolution. **Fix:** Add an explicit "reuses P2.3 `src/capture/threading.ts`" note to P2.5 Technical Notes (mirror the maskAndCap reuse language), or list it in Files-to-Modify if it must be extended.

**P2.5:41 | LOW | Row-mapping omits `result_json` but `turn_end` carries `toolResults` — minor under-capture, but flag as intentional.** P2.5 captures only message text; tool results are P2.3's job. Acceptable, but the spec should state "`toolResults` intentionally not captured here (owned by P2.3 `tool_call` events) to avoid double-write" so a dev doesn't add it. **Fix:** One sentence in scope-out / Technical Notes.

---

## Cross-cutting

**P2.3:48-57 | LOW | Files to Modify lists 7 paths for P2.3 but the canonical-pattern claim means 4 of them (`mask-and-cap.ts`, `embed.ts`, `threading.ts`) are shared infra co-located with P2.3 — fine under the ≤10 rule, but P2.5 then has only 5 files and depends on 3 unlisted P2.3 files at runtime.** Not a violation (cross-story reuse is cited), but the dependency on P2.3's `embed.ts`/`mask-and-cap.ts`/`threading.ts` is a hard build-order coupling not reflected in "Dependency edges (predecessors): P2.1" (line 27, both). P2.5 depends on P2.3, not just P2.1. **Fix:** Add P2.3 to P2.5's dependency edges, or explicitly note "reuses P2.3-authored utils (same writing batch W-C)."

**P2.3:19 / P2.5:19 | LOW | "no stub and no TODO" is asserted but the orphan-run path + start/end correlation (above) are exactly the kind of gap that gets stubbed during dev.** Stated to connect the HIGH findings: the "wiring closed" claim is only credible once the orphan path and `tool_execution_start` correlation are specified; otherwise the dev closes them with a TODO and the claim is false.

---

## Summary

The two specs are well-structured and the canonical-pattern reuse discipline (maskAndCap) is genuinely good. But four structural holes will produce a non-functional or insecure capture spine if implemented as written:

1. **Secrets leak into `event_vec`** (both) — embed input is not the masked output.
2. **Orphan-event path undefined** (both) — INV-OM2 enforced by either crashing or silent data loss.
3. **P2.5 buffering is not provably lossless** — `turn_end`/`agent_end` flush boundaries contradict the idempotency guard.
4. **P2.3 row-mapping references `args`/`duration_ms` not present in `tool_execution_end`** — requires unspecified start/end correlation; `payload_json` would be undefined.

Plus the unserializable-payload crash exposure and fabricated/inconsistent `types.ts` line numbers.

```
HIGH=8 MEDIUM=9 LOW=5
```

(HIGH: P2.3 embed-leak; P2.5 embed-leak; P2.5 missing p99/ONNX constraints; P2.3 unserializable crash; P2.5 unserializable+messageText extraction; orphan-event path [counts once, structural, both]; P2.5 fabricated line numbers; P2.5 buffering losslessness.)
