# ADVERSARIAL CYNICAL REVIEW — A/B Front Pair (P2.9 auth-gated + P2.10 spike-gated)

Method: bmad-review-adversarial-general. Posture: assume the spec is hiding a bug until proven otherwise. Findings are `STORY:LINE | SEVERITY | problem | concrete fix`.

---

## P2.9 — Propose + Human Gate

**P2.9:32,83,95 | LOW | Authorization is asserted but the authorization SOURCE is never named.** Every authZ statement ("a registered authorized actor", "the authorized-actor source", INV-EXP1) refers to an oracle that is never defined: where does the allowlist live, what backs it, who provisions it? A dev can satisfy every AC with `if (actor === "alice") {...}` hardcoded and pass the test suite literally as written (AC1 names `alice`). The gate's authZ is the load-bearing security claim of the whole epic and its trust root is undefined. **Fix:** Add one Technical Note line naming the authorized-actor source (e.g., "actor checked against `experiments.authorized_actors` config / env allowlist `QDS_EXP_ACTORS` — provenance owned by this story") and an AC that an unknown-but-well-formed actor (not the literal "alice") is rejected, so the test can't pass via a hardcoded name.

**P2.9:64,84,94 | MEDIUM | "Same transaction as the status flip" is stated but no transaction boundary owner is named, and the predecessor P2.8 does not own a transaction helper.** INV-EXP2 demands atomic flip+event, but `state-machine.ts` is net-new in THIS story and the Technical Notes never say which layer opens the `db.transaction(fn)` — the research context (§D.4) explicitly warns better-sqlite3/bun:sqlite transactions are sync and must wrap both writes. If `gate.ts` flips the row and `state-machine.ts` emits the event in two separate prepared statements without a shared tx wrapper, the atomicity invariant is violated under crash. **Fix:** Technical Notes should state the emitter and the UPDATE execute inside one `db.transaction(fn)` opened by `gate.ts`/`propose.ts`, citing the §D.4 sync-transaction pattern; add a DoD/test box for "crash between flip and event leaves neither" (or assert single-statement atomicity).

**P2.9:82,83 | HIGH | INV-OM2 (`run_id` non-null on the audit event) is structurally impossible for the gate transition as specified — the gate has no originating run.** INV-OM2 says every transition event carries non-null `run_id` "bound to an originating run context." But the human gate (`experiment approve <id> --actor alice`) is a CLI action by a human — there is NO run in flight. §C `events.run_id` is `NOT NULL`. The schema therefore **cannot** record a gate-transition event without inventing a `run_id`, yet the story mandates one (DoD line 97). This is a real contradiction that will block the e2e demo (AC1 requires the `proposed→approved` event to be written). **Fix:** Resolve the gate-event `run_id` policy explicitly: either (a) the experiment lifecycle gets a synthetic `run_id` (e.g., `experiment:<id>` lifecycle run created at propose time) that all transitions reference, or (b) flag that gate/propose transition events are a documented exception to INV-OM2 and the schema needs a sentinel. The propose transition has the same problem. This must be decided in-spec, not left to the dev.

**P2.9:18,309 | LOW | The "second gate at deploy rejected (§8.2)" rationale is captured in P2.9 Overview prose but NOT pinned as a guard a future dev would trip over.** The Overview says "The §8.2 second gate at deploy was considered and rejected, so this is THE gate" — good — but it lives only in P2.9 prose, while the place a future dev would ADD a second gate is P2.10 (deploy). P2.10:20 does carry the rationale, so this is covered cross-story, but neither story turns it into a negative DoD/test ("deploy path asserts NO authZ check is introduced"). **Fix:** Optional — add a negative DoD box on P2.10 ("deploy introduces no human gate; winner deploys under P2.9 approval — §8.2") so the rejection is test-enforced, not just prose.

**P2.9:34,40 | MEDIUM | Reject path field-setting is inconsistent with the schema/DoD.** AC3 (reject) records `decided_at` and `approved_by='bob'` but NOT `approved_at` — correct, a rejected experiment was never approved. But DoD line 92 says the gate "sets `approved_at`/`approved_by`/`decided_at`" unconditionally for both approve AND reject. Setting `approved_at` on a rejection is semantically wrong and would make a rejected row indistinguishable from approved on the `approved_at` index. **Fix:** Split the DoD: approve sets `approved_at`+`approved_by`+`decided_at`; reject sets `decided_at`+`approved_by` (the deciding actor) only, leaving `approved_at` NULL. Reconcile with the §C column semantics (`approved_by` doubling as "decider" is itself a smell — see next).

**P2.9:34,40,83 | LOW | `approved_by` is overloaded to mean "deciding actor" on rejections.** On reject, AC3 puts `bob` in `approved_by` even though bob rejected. The schema has no `rejected_by`/`decided_by` column, so this is forced, but it means a query for "who approved X" returns the rejecter. **Fix:** Acknowledge the overload explicitly in Technical Notes ("`approved_by` holds the deciding actor for both approve and reject; disambiguate by `status`") so downstream P2.11/meta-optimizer consumers don't misread it.

**P2.9:33 | LOW | Propose AC asserts "exactly one `experiments` row is written" but no idempotency/dedup guard exists against the optimizer proposing the same regression twice.** Research §E P2.9 anti-success is literally "experiments pile in `proposed` (gate abandoned)" — re-running the optimizer over the same runs will write duplicate `proposed` rows for the same skill regression, accelerating the pile-up. There's no `UNIQUE` or "skip if open proposal exists for skill" guard. **Fix:** Add a guard/AC: propose skips (or is rejected) if an open (`proposed`/`approved`/`running`) experiment already exists for the same `skill`+`variant` pair; or explicitly scope-out dedup with rationale.

---

## P2.10 — Execute + Deploy

**P2.10:5,28,98,108 | (PASS) | BLOCKED-BY SPIKE-10 handled correctly.** Header note present (line 5), interface-first framing is genuine (state transitions, winner declaration, baseline snapshot, `confirm_until=+30d` are all unit-testable now per DoD 104–106; E2E variant-tagged-DAG deferred per 108), the `variant` first-class-vs-`ProtocolRun.data` UNKNOWN is correctly attributed to SPIKE-10 (line 20, 53). No ⛔/pending/TBD markers found. This is well-formed.

**P2.10:34,86,106 | HIGH | INV-EXP5 "baseline-before-flip" is unfalsifiable as written because `baseline_metrics_json` content is never defined — a dev can write `{}` and pass.** The make-or-break AC1 and the unit test (DoD 106) only assert that `baseline_metrics_json` is *written* (non-null) before the flip. Nothing specifies WHAT the baseline must contain. An empty object or the literal string `"snapshot"` satisfies every assertion, but then P2.11 (whose entire job is "compare post-deploy metrics vs `baseline_metrics_json`") has nothing to compare against — the rollback precondition is hollow. The whole point of the snapshot is to feed P2.11. **Fix:** Define the baseline shape in Technical Notes (e.g., `{metric, baseline_value, n, window_start, window_end, computed_at}` — the pre-deploy value of the experiment's chosen `metric`), and make AC1/the deploy test assert the snapshot contains the chosen metric's value, not merely that the column is non-null. Cite that P2.11 reads this exact shape.

**P2.10:34,64,86 | MEDIUM | "Baseline snapshot write fails → status stays `analyzed`" is asserted, but baseline + deploy_ref + flip atomicity is not specified as one transaction.** AC1 says if the baseline write fails the flip doesn't happen — but it describes baseline/deploy_ref written "BEFORE" the flip (sequential), not "in the same transaction." Sequential ordering means a crash AFTER baseline write but BEFORE the flip leaves baseline+deploy_ref persisted on an `analyzed` row — then a retry runs the deploy again, re-snapshotting a baseline that now reflects post-partial-deploy state, or double-setting `deploy_ref`. The INV-EXP5 wording ("a flip without a persisted baseline is impossible") only guards one direction. **Fix:** Specify deploy as a single transaction (snapshot + deploy_ref + deployed_at + confirm_until + flip), OR make deploy idempotent on re-run for an `analyzed` row (don't re-snapshot if a baseline already exists for this deploy attempt). Add a test for crash-after-baseline-before-flip.

**P2.10:34,96,108 | MEDIUM | `confirm_until = deployed_at + 30d` — "30d" unit is unstated and `deployed_at` is epoch-ms per §C, inviting an off-by-1000× bug.** §C declares all timestamps epoch-ms (`created_at INTEGER -- epoch ms`). "30d" is prose; a dev computing `deployed_at + 30*24*60*60` (seconds) instead of `*1000` (ms) produces a `confirm_until` 30 seconds out, and P2.11's "30-day cron" fires immediately, auto-rolling-back every deploy. The make-or-break AC asserts the equation but not the unit. **Fix:** State explicitly "`confirm_until = deployed_at + 30*24*60*60*1000` (epoch-ms, matching §C)" and assert the numeric delta (2_592_000_000) in the deploy test, not just "+30d".

**P2.10:36,48,95 | MEDIUM | `winner='none'` "decision margin" is named but never quantified — the winner-declaration logic is undefined.** AC3 and DoD 105 hinge on "neither variant clears the decision margin," but the margin (effect size? p-value? minimum N? minimum delta?) is nowhere specified. Two implementations could declare opposite winners on the same data and both pass. The winner is what gets DEPLOYED — this is the highest-stakes computation in the story. **Fix:** Specify the decision rule in Technical Notes (even a placeholder threshold + "tunable, owned by this story", e.g., "`winner=x` iff `metric(x) - metric(other) ≥ MARGIN` AND `n_per_variant ≥ MIN_N`, else `none`") and assert the boundary (exactly-at-margin → which way?) in the analyze test.

**P2.10:35,38,87 | MEDIUM | Variant config provenance (INV-EXP6) has no storage location in the schema.** AC5/INV-EXP6 require "the variant config provenance is recorded ON THE RUN," but §C `runs` has no provenance column — it has `variant` (a/b) and `experiment_id`, but nothing mapping the run to which `variant_a_ref`/`variant_b_ref` string produced its config. The story says "recorded on the run" but the run table can't hold it. **Fix:** Either map provenance via `runs.variant` + the experiment's `variant_a_ref`/`variant_b_ref` (i.e., provenance is derivable, not stored — then say so and drop "recorded on the run") OR specify the field (e.g., `runs.files_touched_json` is the only free-form column, or this needs a schema GAP callout like the ones research §C lists). As written it asserts a write to a column that doesn't exist.

**P2.10:35,42,84 | MEDIUM | INV-OM2 `run_id`-on-transition-event is again impossible for the `approved→running` and `analyzed→deployed` transitions — and worse here than P2.9.** Same defect as the P2.9 HIGH: the execute/deploy *commands* are operator-invoked, not in-run. INV-OM2 (line 84) demands non-null `run_id` on "every audit transition event." `approved→running` happens before any variant run exists; `analyzed→deployed` happens after runs complete. There is no natural `run_id` for these lifecycle events. **Fix:** Same resolution as P2.9 — adopt a synthetic experiment-lifecycle `run_id` referenced by all transition events across both stories, decided once in the shared `state-machine.ts`. Flag this as a shared cross-story decision.

**P2.10:44,46 | LOW | `n_per_variant` is capped at 50 with no rationale and no resource-cost note, while execute dispatches `2 × N` real swarmclaw runs.** `N=50` → 100 dispatched runs per execute call, each a full swarmclaw DAG run. The cap exists (good) but its basis is unstated; for a net-new execution path the cost/concurrency of 100 parallel dispatches deserves a guard note (rate-limit? sequential vs parallel dispatch?). **Fix:** One Technical Note on dispatch concurrency/cost; confirm whether dispatch is batched or throttled.

**P2.10:42,63 | LOW | `executed_at` set on `approved→running` per Technical Notes, but AC2 (the execute scenario) never asserts `executed_at` is written.** DoD 94 asserts it; AC2 (line 35) only asserts the runs dispatched + tags + the flip. Minor AC/DoD drift. **Fix:** Add `executed_at non-null` to AC2's Then-clause.

---

## State-Machine Integrity (both, cross-cutting)

**P2.9:56 + P2.10:56 | MEDIUM | The two stories author/extend ONE shared `state-machine.ts` legal-transition table but the merge contract between them is implicit.** P2.9 declares legal transitions `proposed→approved|rejected ONLY` (line 56) and tests that EVERY other source state is refused (DoD 98 lists `running`, `analyzed` as illegal). P2.10 then EXTENDS the table to add `approved→running`, `running→analyzed`, `analyzed→deployed` (line 56). If P2.9 ships first and its test asserts `approved→running` is illegal (it's not in P2.9's table), P2.10's extension will break P2.9's test. The "shared with P2.9" note (P2.10:88,100) acknowledges sharing but not the test-compatibility hazard. **Fix:** P2.9's illegal-transition test must scope its refusal assertions to transitions OUT of `approved`/`rejected` via the GATE entrypoint (single-shot), not assert global illegality of `approved→running`; or the legal-transition table must be authored complete (all P2.9–P2.11 edges) in P2.9 with per-entrypoint guards. State the table-ownership/extension contract explicitly.

**Both | LOW | The full §B.2 enum includes `confirming`/`confirmed`/`rolled_back` (P2.11), but neither story states whether the `state-machine.ts` table is authored complete or incrementally.** Tied to the above. If P2.9 authors only its 2 edges and P2.10 adds 3, P2.11 adds the rest — three stories mutating one transition table. **Fix:** Decide and state: complete table authored in P2.9 (recommended — the enum is fully known from §B.2) with entrypoint-level guards enforcing which caller may invoke which edge.

---

## Dependency Integrity

**P2.9 / P2.10 | (PASS) | Dependency edges correct.** P2.9 deps P2.8 (line 26, matches research §E P2.9 "Deps: P2.8"). P2.10 deps P2.9 + SPIKE-10 (line 28, matches research §E P2.10 + §B.4 SPIKE-10 gates E2E of P2.10). Correct.

**P2.9:26 | LOW | P2.9→P2.8 dependency is asserted but the optimizer "scans recent runs" against the operational store (P2.1), not against P2.8's Hindsight retain path.** The optimizer reads `runs`/`events` from the P2.1 store to find regressions; P2.8 is the Hindsight *retain bridge* (outbound to Hindsight banks). The Overview (line 26) justifies the P2.8 edge as "establishes the promotion/retain path the optimizer reads against" — but the optimizer reads the local operational store, so the hard data dependency is P2.1, not P2.8. The P2.8 edge may be a sequencing/sprint-order choice, not a true data dependency. **Fix:** Verify the optimizer's actual read source; if it reads P2.1 `runs`/`events`, the predecessor should cite P2.1 (or both), and the "reads against" justification corrected. A wrong dependency edge mis-sequences the build DAG.

---

## Summary

```
HIGH=3  MEDIUM=9  LOW=10
```

**The three HIGH findings are the blockers:**

1. **P2.9 INV-OM2 vs gate `run_id`** — the audit event for a human-gate transition cannot satisfy `events.run_id NOT NULL` because no run is in flight; the schema and the invariant contradict each other. (Mirrored as a MEDIUM on P2.10 for execute/deploy lifecycle events — same root cause, fix once in shared state machine.)
2. **P2.10 hollow baseline** — `baseline_metrics_json` content is undefined, so `{}` passes every test while leaving P2.11's rollback comparison with nothing to compare. The rollback precondition is asserted but not real.
3. **P2.9 undefined authZ trust root** — the gate's authorization source is never named, so the epic's central security invariant (INV-EXP1) can be satisfied by a hardcoded actor name that the spec literally hands the dev (`alice`).

**What's genuinely solid:** P2.10's SPIKE-10 gating is exemplary (interface-first is real, not a stub-dressed-as-spec; UNKNOWN correctly attributed). The no-auto-approve invariant (INV-EXP3) and the propose-can-only-write-`proposed` guard (AC5) are tight. Dependency edges are correct. The "second gate rejected" rationale survives in both stories' prose so a future dev won't re-add it.

# ADVERSARIAL REVIEW — Promotion Pipeline (P2.7 + P2.8)

Method: bmad-review-adversarial-general. Cynical posture: I assume the spec is hiding a crash-window, a double-fire, or a re-exposure bug until it proves otherwise. Findings reference exact lines.

---

## P2.7 — Nightly Promotion Cron

**P2.7:22,32,88 | HIGH | Crash-mid-run is NOT resume/replay safe — the run-marker is a single binary gate, so a crash AFTER inserting the marker but BEFORE writing the doc permanently strands the date.** The make-or-break AC (line 32) and INV-P27-1 (line 88) treat "marker present ⇒ skip, never re-process" as the safety property. But the `PromotionRun` schema (lines 60-64) has `started_at` + `finished_at` (nullable) + `doc_path` (nullable) — meaning the marker is inserted at start. If the job crashes between marker-insert and doc-write, the next night's re-invocation for that date sees `marker present → skip` and the day is silently lost with NO doc. The spec never states whether the guard keys on "marker exists" or "marker exists AND finished_at != null / doc_path != null." This is the classic partial-run hole. **Fix:** Add an explicit AC: "Given a `promotion_runs` row exists for date D with `finished_at IS NULL` (prior crash), When the job is re-invoked for D, Then it treats the run as incomplete and re-processes (or rolls the stale marker forward), never silently skips a date that produced no doc." Define the idempotency key as completion-state, not mere row-existence, in INV-P27-1.

**P2.7:37,22 | HIGH | The single-instance guard (AC6) describes the OUTCOME but never names the atomicity mechanism — "acquires the run-marker" is hand-waved, and a non-atomic check-then-insert loses the race the AC claims it wins.** AC6 (line 37) and the E2E (line 22, "acquires a single-instance run-marker row") assert that under two overlapping ticks "only one acquires the run-marker and proceeds." But nothing specifies HOW: a `SELECT ... then INSERT` is a TOCTOU race — both ticks read "no marker," both insert, both proceed, double-LLM-call, double-doc. The whole INV-P27-1 guarantee rests on an atomic acquire that the spec leaves implicit. **Fix:** State the atomic primitive explicitly in Guards/limits and AC6: "single-instance acquire is a single atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT(date) DO NOTHING` (or `INSERT OR IGNORE`) where `date` is the PRIMARY KEY / UNIQUE; the tick that affects 0 rows exits." Add `PRIMARY KEY(date)` or a UNIQUE constraint to the `PromotionRun` schema (line 60 currently declares no key/uniqueness on `date`).

**P2.7:60-64 | HIGH | `PromotionRun` schema has no uniqueness/PK on `date` — so "marker row count stays at exactly 1" (AC1, line 32) is unenforceable at the storage layer and relies entirely on application discipline.** The make-or-break AC counts rows (`count stays at exactly 1`). Without a UNIQUE/PK on `date`, two ticks (or one retry) physically CAN insert two rows; the AC would then fail and there is no DB-level backstop. **Fix:** Add `date` as PRIMARY KEY (or UNIQUE) to the `PromotionRun` Zod/DDL and have the DoD assert the constraint exists. This is the same root as the prior two findings — the schema must make the invariant structural, not aspirational.

**P2.7:36,87 | MEDIUM | Secret re-exposure via `error` strings and reasoning text is NOT closed — INV-OM3 (line 87) asserts source rows were "already ≤64KB secret-masked at write," but the consolidation LLM SUMMARY (line 54, the only LLM output) is net-new text that the mask was never applied to in the right order.** The checklist explicitly flags this: raw events were masked at capture, but does consolidation re-expose anything? The events schema (research §C line 148-151) has `error TEXT` and `result_json` — `error` is NOT listed among the masked fields (only `payload_json`/`result_json` are masked per INV-OM3 / research line 111). An LLM cluster summary can paraphrase a secret out of an unmasked `error` string or recombine masked fragments. AC5 (line 36) says "no secret pattern survives," and line 87 says "re-applies the secret-mask," but the ordering is unstated: is the mask applied to the LLM OUTPUT (correct) or only re-applied to the already-masked inputs (useless)? **Fix:** AC5 must assert the mask runs on the FINAL rendered doc body (JSON summaries + prose) AFTER LLM generation, and explicitly include `error` (and any unmasked column) in the masked surface. Add a unit test where a secret lives ONLY in an `error` field and is paraphrased by a stubbed summarizer, asserting it does not survive.

**P2.7:35,57 | MEDIUM | Size-cap truncation order vs. secret-mask is unspecified — truncating a doc mid-token after masking, or masking after truncation, can either re-expose or corrupt.** AC4 (line 35, truncate + `truncated:true`) and AC5 (line 36, mask) are independent ACs with no stated ordering. If truncation slices through a partially-masked region, or if masking runs before truncation and truncation then exposes a previously-elided tail, you get a defect. **Fix:** Pin the pipeline order in Guards/limits: `...→ summarize → render → MASK → CAP/truncate → validate`, and state that the cap is measured on the post-mask bytes (so `doc_bytes ≤ cap` (line 57) is the final masked size).

**P2.7:34,68 | MEDIUM | Idle-night skip threshold is on "event volume" but the marker still gets written with `skipped:true` — does a skipped night BLOCK a later same-date re-run if volume later appears?** AC3 (line 34) writes a marker with `skipped:true, reason below-threshold`. Combined with the binary "marker present ⇒ skip" gate (the AC1 root issue), a night skipped at 02:00 with low volume that then receives late-arriving events (clock skew, backfill) is permanently sealed as skipped. The spec does not say whether a skipped marker is terminal. **Fix:** State explicitly that `skipped:true` markers are terminal-for-that-cron-window by design (acceptable) OR allow re-evaluation; either way, name it. The current ambiguity is a latent data-loss path.

**P2.7:69 | MEDIUM | Cron library is explicitly UNPINNED in the story ("Pin the chosen lib at P2.0") while croner/cron-parser/node-cron all carry different single-fire + Bun-runtime semantics — this defers a make-or-break runtime decision out of the story that depends on it.** Line 69 recommends `croner` but leaves the pin to P2.0, and notes "node-cron ACCEPTABLE only after a Bun-runtime smoke check." If P2.0 pins a lib whose missed-tick/overlap behavior differs from what this story's idempotency story assumes, the assumption silently breaks. The story does correctly say idempotency does NOT rely on the scheduler (good) — but the off-peak single-fire SCHEDULE still does. **Fix:** Either add a DoD box "verify chosen cron lib fires exactly once off-peak under Bun (smoke)" in THIS story, or assert the cron-lib pin is a hard predecessor gate. Right now it floats.

**P2.7:18,295 | LOW | Boundary seam is clean but the doc-on-disk handoff contract to P2.8 is loosely typed — P2.7 writes `doc_path` (line 63) and P2.8 "consumes the P2.7 DailyLearningsDoc" (P2.8 line 55), but neither pins WHO reads the file vs. the in-memory object.** The seam (P2.7 stops at producing the doc; retain is P2.8) is correctly stated (line 18). But P2.8's E2E (line 22, "Take a real P2.7 Daily Learnings doc") implies file-read while P2.7 emits both `doc_path` and a typed object. **Fix:** State the handoff medium once (P2.8 reads the artifact at `doc_path` and re-validates against `DailyLearningsDoc`), so the seam has a typed contract not just a prose boundary.

**P2.7:50-55 | LOW | No mechanism specified for `deduped_count` / dedupe correctness — AC2 (line 33) asserts "dedupe demonstrably reduces cluster member count" but "near-identical signals" is never defined (exact hash? embedding similarity? LLM judgment?).** Without a defined dedupe criterion, AC2's "materially smaller" is untestable and could be gamed by trivial collapse. **Fix:** Name the dedupe key (e.g., normalized payload hash, or cosine over the existing 384-dim `event_vec` above threshold T) so the test is deterministic.

## P2.8 — Hindsight Retain Bridge

**P2.8:32,75 | (verified, well-specified) | The stable dated `documentId` + `updateMode:"replace"` IS both a make-or-break AC (line 32) AND a named invariant (INV-P28-1, line 75), and the missing-documentId failure is explicitly guarded.** Checklist item satisfied: line 75 states "omitting the documentId or using append is forbidden," DoD line 89 has "missing documentId path is rejected/guarded," and the schema (line 47) pins `updateMode: z.literal('replace')`. The single-most-dangerous defect is correctly elevated. No finding — credit where due.

**P2.8:34,57 | HIGH | Partial-failure across the per-bank loop is named but the recovery contract is self-contradictory / underspecified — "a failure retaining to one bank does not leave a partial documentId that breaks the next retry's replace" is asserted but no mechanism makes the two-bank push atomic or safely resumable.** AC3 (line 34) and Guards (line 57) demand that a one-bank failure "does not silently skip the other or leave a partial documentId that breaks the next retry's replace." But the two banks are SEPARATE Hindsight calls (no multi-bank retain, line 34) — there is no transaction across them. If qds-product `replace` succeeds and qds-meta throws, you are in a genuinely partial state. The spec asserts the bad outcome is forbidden but never says HOW: does the bridge retry the failed bank? Surface and abort (leaving product updated, meta stale until next night's `replace` overwrites)? Because both banks use the SAME dated documentId with `replace`, a next-night re-run DOES self-heal each bank independently (replace is idempotent per bank) — which is actually fine — but the AC's phrasing "partial documentId that breaks the next retry's replace" implies a fear that isn't mechanically possible with per-bank replace, OR points at a real concern the spec hasn't pinned. **Fix:** State the recovery model explicitly: "each bank's `replace` is independently idempotent; on one-bank failure the bridge surfaces the error AND the partially-applied bank is self-corrected by the next run's `replace` (no cross-bank rollback needed)." Then AC3's "partial documentId" clause should be reworded to what's actually being guarded (don't leave one bank un-pushed without raising).

**P2.8:32,22 | MEDIUM | "Querying each bank returns exactly one document for date D" assumes Hindsight `retain(...replace, documentId=X)` is itself idempotent/atomic — but the spec never cites that guarantee from the retain() contract; it's assumed.** The make-or-break AC (line 32) and E2E (line 22) rest entirely on Hindsight's `replace` semantics deduplicating by `documentId`. Research §D.5 (line 240) supports this ("Idempotency = stable documentId ... Use updateMode:replace"), so the assumption is grounded — but the STORY never asserts it as a depended-upon property of the P1.11 client, so if P1.11's wrapper drops/renames `documentId` the failure is silent. **Fix:** Add a DoD/contract assertion: "verifies the P1.11 `hindsight-client.retain()` actually forwards `documentId` + `updateMode` to the MCP channel (not dropped) — a passthrough test, since the whole idempotency guarantee delegates to it." This closes the cross-epic trust gap to P1.11.

**P2.8:35,52-53,76 | MEDIUM | Tag VALUES are never validated against an enumerated vocabulary — the tag KEYS (module/knowledge_type/severity ; skill/outcome/quality_dim) are correct, but `z.record(z.string(), z.string())` (line 52) accepts ANY value, so a misrouted `knowledge_type:"banana"` passes the contract.** AC4 (line 35) and INV-P28-2 (line 76) assert "correct tag scoping," and the keys match research §D.5 / QDS-MEM-001 §6.2 (research line 240). But the schema validates only string-typed values, not the controlled vocabulary. Routing per §6.2 (line 33, AC2) is the thing that assigns these, but nothing checks the OUTPUT is a legal §6.2 value. **Fix:** Replace the open `z.record` with per-bank tag schemas using `z.enum([...])` for the closed-vocab fields (at minimum `knowledge_type`, `severity`, `outcome`, `quality_dim`), or add a DoD test that asserts produced tag values are members of the §6.2 vocabulary. As written, INV-P28-2 ("no anonymous/untagged writes") catches missing tags but not WRONG tag values.

**P2.8:33,34 | MEDIUM | Routing/precedence (§6.2) "no section misrouted" (AC2) has no defined behavior for a section that matches BOTH precedence rules or NEITHER — the precedence is named but the conflict/orphan resolution is not.** AC2 (line 33) asserts every product-class section → qds-product and meta-class → qds-meta "with no section misrouted." But §6.2 is "precedence" rules (line 18, 55) — precedence implies overlapping matches resolved by priority. What happens to a Daily Learnings cluster that is BOTH a product pattern AND a skill outcome? Duplicated to both banks (acceptable, but then "exactly one document per bank" still holds per-bank — fine) or assigned by precedence to one? And a cluster matching NEITHER — silently dropped (data loss) or defaulted? Unspecified. **Fix:** Add an AC or Guards line: "a section matching multiple precedence classes routes by §6.2 priority order; a section matching no class routes to {default bank | is flagged, never silently dropped}." Otherwise clusters can vanish between P2.7's doc and Hindsight.

**P2.8:58,303 | (verified) | SPIKE-9/D7 status is correctly stated — line 58 "resolves SPIKE-9 (D7) — the bridge is a batch nightly retain, NOT a live per-event hook; no spike is open against this story," matching research §B.4 (line 129) and §E P2.8 (line 303).** Boundary "does not do the consolidation (P2.7 owns that)" is also clean (line 18). Per-bank loop / no multi-bank retain is correct (research line 240). P1.11 cross-epic dep is cited (lines 18, 24, 26, 55, 303). No finding.

**P2.8:36,5,1 | LOW | Spec Reference cites "QDS-MEM-003 §7.1–7.2" and QDS-MEM-001 §6.2 but the secret-mask claim (AC5, line 36) leans entirely on P2.7 upstream — if P2.7's mask has the `error`-field gap I flagged above, P2.8's INV-OM3 (line 74) inherits the hole and "verifies" a mask that didn't cover everything.** AC5/INV-OM3 (lines 36, 74) say the bridge "verifies the upstream P2.7 secret-mask before retain." But verification of an incomplete mask is false assurance. **Fix:** P2.8's verification should be an independent secret-pattern scan on the retained body (defense-in-depth), not merely trusting P2.7 ran. Phrase line 74 as "re-scan, not re-trust."

**P2.8:18 | LOW | "re-pushing the same doc replaces rather than duplicates" is proven only for the happy two-bank doc — there is no AC for a doc whose ROUTING changed between runs (e.g., P2.7 re-emitted date D with different clusters).** If P2.7's marker-incomplete reprocessing (my P2.7 HIGH finding) regenerates date D's doc with different content, the `replace` correctly overwrites — good — but a section that moved from qds-product to qds-meta between versions leaves a STALE entry in qds-product (the old documentId still there, `replace` only overwrites the bank you push to). **Fix:** Note that `replace` is per-(bank,documentId); if a date's content re-routes across banks between runs, the prior bank retains a stale copy unless explicitly cleared. At minimum document this edge; ideally always push BOTH banks every run (even empty) so `replace` clears a now-empty bank's prior entry.

---

## Cross-pair / boundary

**PAIR:P2.7→P2.8 | MEDIUM | The end-to-end idempotency story has a SEAM GAP: P2.7 idempotency is keyed on the `promotion_runs` marker (DB row), P2.8 idempotency is keyed on the Hindsight `documentId` — but NOTHING links "P2.7 marker says done" to "P2.8 retain succeeded."** If P2.7 completes (marker `finished_at` set, doc written) and P2.8 then crashes mid-retain, what re-triggers P2.8? P2.8 has no marker of its own. The next cron night processes a NEW date; date D's doc is written but never retained. Neither story owns "P2.7 done but P2.8 never ran." **Fix:** Either P2.8 records its own retain-completion marker (e.g., `retained_at` on the `promotion_runs` row) so a doc-written-but-not-retained date is detectable and replayable, OR explicitly state the cron-runner invokes P2.7-then-P2.8 in one tick and a P2.8 failure leaves the P2.7 marker incomplete to force full replay. Right now the two idempotency guards don't compose into an end-to-end exactly-once.

---

## Tally

`HIGH=5 MEDIUM=8 LOW=5`

**HIGH breakdown:** P2.7 crash-mid-run silently strands a date (marker-existence vs completion-state); P2.7 single-instance acquire atomicity unspecified (TOCTOU); P2.7 `PromotionRun` lacks PK/UNIQUE on `date` so the count-==1 invariant is unenforceable; P2.8 per-bank partial-failure recovery contract self-contradictory/underspecified.

The two most load-bearing structural fixes are both on P2.7 and share one root: **the run-marker must be a completion-state ledger with a UNIQUE/PK on `date` and an atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` acquire** — that single change closes the TOCTOU race, the count-==1 enforceability gap, and (with a `finished_at`/`doc_path` not-null guard) the crash-mid-run strand. P2.8 is in markedly better shape: its single most dangerous defect (omitted documentId) is correctly elevated to make-or-break + named invariant + guarded DoD; its remaining gaps are the cross-bank partial-failure recovery and uncontrolled tag-value vocabulary.

**P2.9:26 | MEDIUM | P2.9→P2.8 dependency edge is semantically questionable / contradicts the Overview.** P2.9 declares predecessor **P2.8** ("Hindsight retain bridge — establishes the promotion/retain path the optimizer reads against"). But P2.9's actual data source is the *operational `events`/`runs` store* (P2.9 AC2 line 33 "a set of recent runs with a measurable skill regression" — read from P2.1's store, not from Hindsight). Nothing in P2.9's implementation reads from qds-product/qds-meta banks (P2.8's output). The real hard dependency of P2.9 is **P2.1** (the experiments table + events store) and arguably the capture stories. The §F/§B.4 chain has P2.9→P2.8 as a *serialization* convenience, but the prompt's own expected DAG lists "P2.9→P2.8". So P2.9 matches the declared DAG — but the *rationale string* ("the promotion/retain path the optimizer reads against") is false: the optimizer reads the operational store, not the retain output. **Concrete fix:** Either correct P2.9's dependency rationale (P2.9 truly needs P2.1's `experiments` table + recall, with P2.8 as a soft/ordering predecessor), or justify why the optimizer must read Hindsight banks. As written, P2.9 cites a predecessor whose output it never consumes, while omitting P2.1 whose `experiments` DDL it depends on entirely (P2.9:40 "Uses the §C `experiments` DDL verbatim").

**P2.11:26 | LOW | P2.11 lists three predecessors (P2.10, P2.8, P1.11); §B.4 lists only P2.10.** §B.4:129 / prompt expected: "P2.11→P2.10". P2.11 adds "P2.8 / P1.11 (the `retain()` path reused for the qds-meta finding)." This is *more* complete than the DAG (P2.11 genuinely reuses the retain path), not contradictory. Acceptable — extra-but-justified, consistent with P2.8/P1.11 owning retain. No fix required, but note §B.4's DAG is under-specified vs the story.

## 5. Shared-util ownership (maskAndCap)

**MEDIUM | Filename/path drift for the shared mask util across P2.3/P2.4/P2.6.** P2.3 authors it (GAP #4) at **`src/capture/mask-and-cap.ts`** (P2.3:52, exporting `maskAndCap`). Consumers:
- P2.5:42 imports it correctly: "import P2.3 `maskAndCap` from **`src/capture/mask-and-cap.ts`**" ✅
- **P2.4:74 references the wrong path:** "reuse `src/capture/**mask-util.ts**` (P2.3-owned 64KB cap/mask)" — file name is `mask-util.ts`, not `mask-and-cap.ts`. ❌
- **P2.6:58 references the wrong path:** "reuse `src/capture/**mask-util.ts**` (P2.3-owned cap/mask)" — same wrong name. ❌

P2.4 and P2.6 cite a file (`mask-util.ts`) that P2.3 never creates (P2.3 creates `mask-and-cap.ts`). **Concrete fix:** Change P2.4:74 and P2.6:58 to `src/capture/mask-and-cap.ts` to match the P2.3-authored path. This is the single most concrete "consumer points at non-existent shared file" defect — a developer following P2.4/P2.6 verbatim would import a path that doesn't exist.

**LOW | function-name vs file-name:** P2.6 says "cap util" / "P2.3 cap util" generically; P2.3/P2.5 use the function name `maskAndCap`. P2.4:103 says "the shared P2.3 util" without naming `maskAndCap`. Recommend P2.4/P2.6 name the export `maskAndCap` explicitly for consistency. **No story re-defines/re-implements the util** — P2.5:42 and P2.5:63 are emphatic about reuse ("no local re-implementation"). ✅ Ownership is singular (P2.3); only the *path string* drifts.

**LOW | embed util reuse path.** P2.3 authors `src/capture/embed.ts` (P2.3:53). P2.5:43 reuses "P2.3 `embed.ts`" ✅. P2.4 (node capture) computes no embedding and doesn't reference embed.ts (consistent with §1 finding that node events aren't embedded). P2.6 also no embed (outcome events not embedded). Internally consistent with the no-embed-for-node/outcome pattern — but that pattern itself is never documented (see §1 MEDIUM).

## 6. Naming / slug / IDs

**PASS.** Headings all use dot form `# P2.N:` (P2.0:1 through P2.12:1). Filenames all hyphen `p2-n-...md`. All 13 story IDs unique (P2.0–P2.12, no gaps, no dupes). Titles unique. ✅

**LOW | P2.0 title scope vs filename.** Filename `p2-0-toolchain-bootstrap.md`; heading "P2.0: Vendor pi-mem Substrate + Pin Dual-Runtime Toolchain"; §F:248 calls it "Toolchain bootstrap." All three describe the same story; the filename slug ("toolchain-bootstrap") under-represents the vendor-substrate half but is not a conflict. Acceptable.

## 7. Spike attribution

**PASS with one verification.**
- **SPIKE-10** carried by exactly **P2.4** (header line 5 "BLOCKED-BY SPIKE-10") and **P2.10** (header line 6 "BLOCKED-BY SPIKE-10"). ✅ No other story claims SPIKE-10. P2.9 (the propose half of A/B) correctly does NOT carry SPIKE-10 (it has no variant-tagged execution). ✅
- **SPIKE-11** informs **P2.1** non-blocking (P2.1:97 "informational, NOT a blocker"; P2.1:18 "carries SPIKE-11"; DoD line 144 "recorded as de-risk evidence, non-blocking"). ✅ Also referenced de-risk-only in P2.0:34 (AC3 "records the SPIKE-11 native-load risk surface — de-risk only, does not block this story"). **LOW note:** SPIKE-11 appears in BOTH P2.0 and P2.1. §B.4:129 says "SPIKE-11 informs P2.1." §F:258 says P2.1 carries SPIKE-11. P2.0 also touches it (the dual-runtime `vec_version()` check). Not a misattribution (both legitimately validate the native load), but two stories now "own" SPIKE-11 evidence. **Concrete fix (optional):** Designate P2.1 as the SPIKE-11 evidence owner and downgrade P2.0's mention to "preliminary load check," to avoid two stories both claiming to resolve the same spike.
- **SPIKE-9** cited as RESOLVED in **P2.7** (line 10 "SPIKE-9 (resolved: bridge is batch cron, not live hook)") and **P2.8** (line 58 "resolves SPIKE-9 (D7)… no spike is open against this story"). ✅ Consistent with §B.4:129 "SPIKE-9 RESOLVED." No story treats SPIKE-9 as open. ✅

**P2.4:5 vs P2.10:6 | LOW | SPIKE-10 blocking-scope wording differs.** P2.4:5 "BLOCKED-BY SPIKE-10 (… shape unknown)" — implies the whole story is blocked, but the body (line 20, 26) clarifies interface-first/E2E-gated. P2.10:6 "BLOCKED-BY SPIKE-10 (variant-tagged DAG execution). Ships interface + unit tests first; full E2E when seam confirmed" — the header itself scopes it to E2E. §B.4 says SPIKE-10 "gates P2.4 + **E2E of** P2.10." So P2.4's header should mirror P2.10 and say E2E-gated, not bare "BLOCKED-BY." Both bodies are correct; only P2.4's header overstates the block. **Concrete fix:** Align P2.4:5 header to "BLOCKED-BY SPIKE-10 for live tap + E2E; ships interface + unit tests first" to match P2.10:6 and §B.4's "E2E of" scoping.

---

## Severity tally

```
HIGH=0 MEDIUM=6 LOW=12
```

**The 6 MEDIUMs (highest-value fixes), ranked:**
1. **P2.4:74 + P2.6:58** — shared mask util path drift (`mask-util.ts` vs the real `mask-and-cap.ts`); consumers point at a non-existent file. *(Most concrete, cheapest fix.)*
2. **P2.2:62-65** — P2.2 migration files don't author P2.1's FTS5 table + 16 indexes; ownership seam leaves that DDL with no runtime applier.
3. **P2.9:82 / P2.11:61** — INV-OM2 asserts non-null `run_id` on experiment-lifecycle audit events that have no legitimate run context (propose aggregates many runs; gate is a human CLI action).
4. **P2.11 vs P2.9/P2.10** — P2.11 reimplements transition logic in `src/optimizer/experiment-store.ts` instead of extending the shared `src/experiments/state-machine.ts`, bypassing the "single source of truth" legal-transition table.
5. **P2.9:26** — P2.9 cites P2.8 as predecessor with a false rationale ("the path the optimizer reads against"); P2.9 actually reads the P2.1 operational store, and omits P2.1 from its declared edges.
6. **P2.4/P2.6 (event_vec)** — no story documents that `node`/`outcome` events are NOT embedded into `event_vec` (only `tool_call`/`message` are); silent KNN-coverage gap.

No HIGH findings: the load-bearing contracts (the §C DDL, the 9-value experiments status enum, the four INV-OM definitions, and every build-DAG dependency edge) are byte-consistent across all 13 stories. The inconsistencies are in shared-util path strings, DDL-applier ownership seams, and run_id/state-machine reuse semantics — all fixable with localized edits, none requiring a schema or enum change.

All present.

**R14 (P2.8) — VERIFIED RESOLVED.** Per-bank replace idempotent + self-heal (AC#3, line 66), both banks every run (AC#3, line 34, line 56-57), reworded partial AC (AC#3), z.enum closed-vocab (lines 46-51, AC#4), multi-class→precedence / no-class→flagged (AC#2, line 65), re-scan secrets (AC#5, INV-OM3), P1.11 passthrough test (line 101), retained_at stamp (AC#6, INV-P28-3). documentId kept (line 18, AC#1). All present.

**R15 (P2.12) — VERIFIED RESOLVED.** "retention floor/grace window" (no false reversible — line 18, INV-RPL2), one-tx multi-table delete + zero-orphan AC (AC#3), running-run exclusion + AC (AC#4), dryRun + max-rows cap (AC#5), replay read-only + dedicated metrics row (AC#6, INV-RPL1), dangling-parent + cycle threat model (line 59), perf <200ms N≥1000 warm citing `(run_id,created_at)` (AC#1). All present.

Now the new-issue hunt and cross-seam composition checks.

## Cross-seam: P2.7↔P2.8 exactly-once (retained_at)

The seam composes structurally but has a gap. P2.7 stamps `doc_path`; P2.8 stamps `retained_at`. A "doc-written-but-not-retained" date is detectable (`doc_path` set, `retained_at` NULL). **But neither story owns the detection/replay scan.** P2.8 AC#6 says the gap is "detectable and replayable" — yet no story contains a scan that finds `doc_path IS NOT NULL AND retained_at IS NULL` and re-pushes. P2.8's idempotency is driven by P2.7's cron invoking the bridge inline, but if P2.7 completes (writes doc, marks `finished_at`) and the retain step later fails, the next nightly cron acquires a NEW date's marker and never revisits the stranded prior date. This is the same class of bug R12 fixed for P2.7's own crash path — but the retain seam reintroduces it one layer up.

## Cross-seam: P2.12 retention vs FK (R2) consistency

Consistent. R2 says no CASCADE, P2.12 deletes events+event_vec+event_edges explicitly in one tx — matches. R6 scope (only tool_call/message embedded) is correctly noted (P2.12 line 18). No contradiction found.

Here are the residual and new findings.

---

**p2-8-hindsight-retain-bridge.md:37 | HIGH | The P2.7↔P2.8 exactly-once seam is *detectable* but not *recoverable* — no story owns a recovery scan for `doc_path IS NOT NULL AND retained_at IS NULL`. If P2.7 writes the doc + sets `finished_at` but the retain fails (or the process dies between doc-write and retain), the next nightly cron acquires a NEW date's marker and never revisits the stranded date. AC#6/INV-P28-3 claim "detectable and replayable" but nothing replays it. | Add to P2.8 (or P2.7's cron-runner) an eligible-scan that, before/after processing today's date, picks up any prior date with `doc_path` set and `retained_at` NULL and re-invokes `RetainBridge.push` for it (mirrors R12's crashed-date reprocess). Add an AC: "Given date D-1 has `doc_path` set but `retained_at` NULL (prior retain failure), When the next nightly run executes, Then it re-pushes D-1 and stamps `retained_at`."**

**p2-8-hindsight-retain-bridge.md:18,34 | HIGH | `retained_at` is stamped "only after BOTH banks succeed" (AC#6, INV-P28-3) but AC#3 mandates that on one-bank failure the bridge "surfaces the error and still attempts the other," relying on "next run's `replace` to self-heal." These compose into a non-atomic partial state with no ledger record: if qds-product succeeds and qds-meta fails, `retained_at` stays NULL (correct), so next run re-pushes BOTH banks — re-pushing qds-product is idempotent via `replace` (fine). But there is no guard that the bank routing is *deterministic across runs* — if the doc content differs on re-read (it shouldn't, it's the same artifact), or if routing precedence is nondeterministic for a multi-class section, the re-push could land the section in a different bank, leaving a stale entry in the original bank that `replace` won't clear (different documentId scope is same, but the *section* moved banks). | State explicitly that routing is a pure deterministic function of the (immutable) artifact so re-push is bit-identical; OR add an AC that re-pushing after partial failure produces byte-identical per-bank content. The "both banks every run even when empty" rule (line 34) covers the empty case but not the *moved-section* case.**

**p2-7-nightly-promotion-cron.md:73 | MEDIUM | Crash-reprocess does `DELETE the incomplete row, then re-acquire` (line 73-74). This DELETE-then-INSERT is itself a TOCTOU window: two overlapping ticks both seeing the incomplete row could both DELETE then both attempt re-acquire; the `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` re-acquire is safe, but the interleaving `DELETE` from instance B can wipe instance A's freshly-acquired row (A INSERTs, B DELETEs A's row, B INSERTs) — now two instances believe they own date D. The original acquire closes TOCTOU via atomic INSERT, but the reprocess path reopens it with a non-atomic DELETE+INSERT. | Make reprocess atomic: a single conditional `UPDATE promotion_runs SET started_at=?, finished_at=NULL WHERE date=? AND finished_at IS NULL AND <crashed-predicate>` that returns affected-rows; the 0-row loser exits. Never DELETE a marker another tick may concurrently own.**

**p2-8-hindsight-retain-bridge.md:34 | MEDIUM | AC#3 says on one-bank failure "surfaces the error and still attempts the other" — but the bridge must NOT stamp `retained_at` if either fails (INV-P28-3 requires both). The story does not state what the *return/throw* contract is on partial failure, so the caller (P2.7 cron) cannot distinguish "fully retained" from "partially retained, retry next run." If P2.7's cron treats any non-throw as success it may mark the night done. | Specify P2.8's failure contract: `push(doc)` throws (or returns a failure result) when either bank fails, leaving `retained_at` NULL, and the caller does not advance. Add to AC#3 the post-condition "on partial failure `retained_at` remains NULL."**

**p2-7-nightly-promotion-cron.md:33,83 | MEDIUM | Dedup AC#2 asserts `deduped_count > 0` as a make-or-break-adjacent binary, but the deterministic key has two branches (cosine ≥ T for embedded `tool_call`/`message`; payload-hash for `node`/`outcome`). The AC and test (line 123) only exercise near-dupe collapse generically; there is no assertion that the *cosine branch* and the *hash branch* are each independently tested. A test that seeds only embedded near-dupes passes AC#2 while the hash-fallback branch is unverified (and `node`/`outcome` events are the ones with NO event_vec per R6, so the hash path is the only dedup they get). | Split the unit test into two arms: (a) embedded near-dupes collapse via cosine≥T, (b) non-embedded (`node`/`outcome`) exact-payload dupes collapse via normalized-hash. ## Summary — QDS-MEM-003 sprint planning complete

13 enterprise-grade stories (P2.0–P2.12, 44 pts), ≤2 per subagent, full rigor, no shortcuts.

**Phase 1 — research:** 5 parallel agents, confidence 0.93. Retargeted for non-standard epic (template = local P-numbered stories, design = `operational-memory-design.md` + `-impl-plan.md §D`, source = local `claude-mem` + `pi-mono` + `swarmclaw`).

**Phase 2 — writing:** 7 agents (W-A…W-G), dual output, all stories `phase-2/stories/p2-*.md`.

**Phase 2.5 — atomicity:** PASS, 0 BLOCK. Every story: norm ≤200, SP ≤8, FtM ≤10, Sec-Inv present, 1 make-or-break, e2e DoD box.

**Phase 3 — adversarial loop converged (3 iterations):**

| Iter | HIGH | MED |
|---|---|---|
| 1 (8 reviewers → 7 fixers) | 41 | 63 |
| 2 (4 verifiers → 5 fixers) | 4 | 12 |
| 3 (convergence verify) | **0** | **0** |

Domain checklists fired: migration, secrets/masking, auth-gate, deserialization, retention.

**Structural catches killed pre-CR:** INV-OM2 run_id contradiction (synthetic lifecycle/orphan runs), secret-leak-into-event_vec, DDL-applier seam, A/B `winner=none` dead-end + baseline shape + state-machine SoT, promotion exactly-once (documentId + recovery scan + atomic marker), retention orphan/in-flight safety.

**Wrap-up:** branch `feature/epic-qds-mem-003-stories`, commit `3fb6642`, pushed, PR #23 → `main` (no `dev` branch exists; repo default is main). sprint-status.yaml phase-2 block (`ready-for-dev`), README index linked. Adversarial log in PR body.

assistant: Handoff prompt below — copy-paste into a fresh session at repo root. Self-contained: locates this session's transcript, converts to the `/reflect`-compatible format, writes it, verifies.

---

```
TASK: Save the most recent qds-sprint-planning session transcript into the learnings
directory so /reflect can extract learnings from it. This is a mechanical archival task.

CONTEXT (what that session did, for the transcript header):
- Repo: /Users/steve/QDS-Factory
- Ran /skills:qds-sprint-planning for epic QDS-MEM-003 (Operational Memory Substrate),
  Track A backend, "no more than two stories per subagent, full enterprise rigor."
- Output: 13 stories P2.0–P2.12 (~44 pts) in _bmad-output/sprint-artifacts/phase-2/stories/
- Gates: atomicity audit PASS (0 BLOCK); adversarial review converged over 3 iterations
  (HIGH 41→4→0, MEDIUM 63→12→0).
- Wrap-up: branch feature/epic-qds-mem-003-stories, commit 3fb6642, PR #23 → main.

STEPS:

1. Find this project's Claude Code transcript directory. Try, in order:
     ls -t ~/.claude/projects/-Users-steve-QDS-Factory/*.jsonl 2>/dev/null
     ls -t ~/.claude/projects/*QDS-Factory*/*.jsonl 2>/dev/null
   The QDS-MEM-003 planning session is the most recent (or 2nd-most-recent if THIS
   archival session created a newer file). Pick the largest recent .jsonl whose content
   mentions "qds-sprint-planning" and "QDS-MEM-003":
     grep -l "QDS-MEM-003" ~/.claude/projects/*QDS-Factory*/*.jsonl | xargs ls -S | head
   If multiple candidates, print each file's first user message + size and ask me to confirm
   which one BEFORE writing.

2. Create the target dir:
     mkdir -p /Users/steve/QDS-Factory/.claude/learnings/qds-sprint-planning/transcripts

3. Convert the chosen .jsonl into a role-tagged .md. Each jsonl line is a JSON object;
   emit one block per turn with role markers /reflect parses (user:/assistant:/system:).
   Use jq if available:
     SRC=<chosen.jsonl>
     OUT=/Users/steve/QDS-Factory/.claude/learnings/qds-sprint-planning/transcripts/2026-06-19_qds-mem-003-planning.md
     {
       echo "# 2026-06-19 qds-sprint-planning — QDS-MEM-003 (Track A, 13 stories)"
       echo
       jq -r 'select(.message.role!=null) |
              "\(.message.role): " +
              (if (.message.content|type)=="string" then .message.content
               else ([.message.content[]? | (.text // .content // "")] | join("\n")) end)' "$SRC"
     } > "$OUT"
   If jq is unavailable or the schema differs, inspect 2-3 lines of the .jsonl first
   (head -3 "$SRC" | python3 -m json.tool on each), adapt the field paths, then convert.
   Requirements for the output file: extension .md; role markers user:/assistant:/system:
   at line starts; readable plain text (strip tool-call noise if huge, but KEEP the
   planning decisions, the adversarial findings/tallies, and the resolution choices —
   those are the learnings signal).

4. Verify:
     test -s "$OUT" && wc -l "$OUT" && grep -cE '^(user|assistant|system):' "$OUT"
   Expect a non-empty file with many role markers.

5. Do NOT commit it unless I ask (learnings transcripts are usually local).
   Report the final path + line count + role-marker count.

DO NOT fabricate transcript content. If you cannot locate the .jsonl, stop and tell me
where you looked — I will point you at the file.
```

---

Note: `/reflect` reads any `.md`/`.txt`/`.transcript` in that dir with role markers — the conversion just makes the raw jsonl parseable. If the new session can't find the jsonl, fallback is to paste the session text manually into a `.md` there.
